<meta>This is the title page of a 17th-century alchemical treatise published in Amsterdam. It introduces the work's primary metaphor, the "Green Lion," a term steeped in the symbolic language of early modern chemistry.</meta>

### T H E
# Green Lion ,
<note>original: "De Groene Leeuw." In alchemy, the Green Lion is a multi-layered symbol. It often represents "vitriol" (sulfuric acid) or the mineral antimony, but it more broadly signifies the raw, "living" state of matter that has the power to dissolve and purify other substances, particularly gold.</note>
## OR THE LIGHT
### of the
## <term>PHILOSOPHERS</term>.
<note>The term "Philosopher" in this context refers to "Hermetic Philosophers" or alchemists—those who sought to understand the hidden laws of nature and the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

To the right, a winged figure (an angel or Cupid) holding a bow looks up and points toward a radiant sun with a face. The winged figure is marked with various planetary symbols: Mercury (☿) on the forehead, Venus (♀) and Mars (♂) on the right arm and shoulder, and Jupiter (♃) and Saturn (♄) on the left arm and wing area. <meta>This page is the beginning of a formal dedication. The signature * 3 indicates it is the third leaf of the introductory matter. ## To the Highly Noble and Well-born Lord,
### MY LORD
# HANS ALBRECHT
## von TREILEBEN,
### Nobleman of His Most Serene Majesty the King of Sweden.

->MY LORD,<-

*Since I well know that the radiance of the Sun will not be obscured by the shadow of the Stars, and that the Sea is not increased by all the Rivers that continually flow into it; so I also confess that Your Honor has no need of my praise, as you are already sufficiently praised in every way through your innate virtues. The reason, then, that has moved me to dedicate this work of mine to Your Honor, is because I am certain that it will come into the hands of a singular Lover of Nature <note>In the 17th century, a "Lover of Nature" (Lief-hebber van de Natuur) referred to someone interested in natural philosophy, or what we now call science.</note>, and one who daily studies <note>Original: "onder-", the first part of "onderzoekt" (examines/studies).</note> the works of the Philosophers <note>The term "Philosopher" here refers to "Natural Philosophers," the precursors to modern scientists who studied the physical laws of the universe.</note>...*

<summary>This page contains a formal dedication to Hans Albrecht von Treileben, a nobleman in the service of the King of Sweden, praising his character and his intellectual interest in the natural world.</summary>
<keywords>Hans Albrecht von Treileben, King of Sweden, dedication, natural philosophy, nobility, patronage</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the dedication from the previous leaf. The word "foekt" at the beginning of this page completes the word "onderzoekt" (examines/studies) from the previous page's final line.</meta>

*...examines; and although these writings have not issued from the pen of a philosopher, but merely from that of a student of nature, who constantly seeks to follow her through the art of <term>Fire</term> <note>Original: $\triangle$</note> and <term>Water</term> <note>Original: $\nabla$</note>; nonetheless, I have dared to embolden myself so far as to bring these before Your Honor, hoping that they shall not be unwelcome, but rather favorably received.*

*The small light that God the Lord has given me, I have not wished to place under a bushel <note>A reference to the biblical parable in Matthew 5:15: "Neither do men light a candle, and put it under a bushel, but on a candlestick."</note>, nor bury my talent in the earth, but rather share it with the world for the general benefit. I do not doubt that all true friends of the most glorious art of <term>Alchemy</term> <note>Alchemy: the historical practice of attempting to transform base metals into gold and discover a universal medicine.</note> and of <term>Hermetic Philosophy</term> <note>Hermetic Philosophy: a tradition based on writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, focusing on the relationship between the human (the microcosm) and the universe (the macrocosm).</note> shall be able to find satisfaction herein; for I have described nothing other than what has become known to me through my own manipulation and labor. And it is certain that this excellent art consists of nothing else but Water and Salt; which I know is by no means unknown to Your Honor: for the <term>Water</term> <note>Original: $\nabla$</note> is from Salt, and Salt is produced by the Water...*

<meta>catchword: voort-</meta>

<summary>The author presents his work as a practical guide to alchemy and Hermetic philosophy, emphasizing that his knowledge comes from hands-on experience and the fundamental elements of water and salt.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Hermetic philosophy, fire, water, salt, biblical allusion, experimental knowledge, Hans Albrecht von Treileben</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the dedication from page 3. The word "voortgebragt" (produced/brought forth) at the top of this page completes the sentence from the previous page: "and Salt is produced by the Water..."</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>
<sig>* 4</sig>

...produced, and distributed to us. <term>Salt</term> is made by the power of God, and also fulfills that which may be commanded of it; namely, to season with salt, which is to say: when food is not salty, one must season it with Salt and Vinegar, so that it might then obtain a good flavor. Salt is the Fountain of <term>Water</term>, and Water is the Mother of <term>Air</term>, in which <term>Fire</term> has its dwelling; and all these proceed from one Nature, which has been established from all eternity by Almighty God.

I would not wish to present such arguments to Your Honor, were I not aware that you are a singular lover of the <term>Art of the Ancient Sages</term> <note>A common historical reference to alchemy and the pursuit of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. Moreover, by the generous hand of God, you have been endowed with an uncommon and praiseworthy knowledge: the ability even to walk beneath the waters <note>This likely refers to early diving technology or a diving bell, a feat of engineering the author finds comparable to alchemical mastery over the elements.</note>; and by such means to retrieve many riches which, through shipwrecks and other accidents, have been swallowed by the sea and were considered lost. You bring these once more to the light of day, so that they may afterwards—contrary to human expectation—still serve for use and benefit,

<note>Vocabulary used in this section: Salt, God's, Vinegar, Fountain, Water, Mother, Air, Fire, Nature, Art of the Ancient Sages (Alchemy), Shipwrecks.</note>

<meta>catchword: waer</meta>

*For which one may then thank God with all one’s heart; since those riches, without your means* <note>Referring to the diving machine or "diving bell" mentioned on the previous page.</note> *would otherwise have had to remain buried forever, sunken in the depths of the Sea.*

*If this, my submissive respect, may not be unpleasantly received by* **Your** *Honor, then I shall henceforth apply myself all my life to strive, on every occasion, to offer all my ability and service to your pleasure; and at all times, without ceasing, demonstrate with what great heartfelt affection I shall endeavor to prove my gratitude for the favors you have shown toward me, and how faithfully I am and wish to remain,*

### MY LORD,

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page begins the author's preface to the work "The Green Lion," following the formal dedication to his patron. The catchword "de" at the bottom leads into the discussion of peace on the following page.</meta>

* 5

## PREFACE
->Regarding the<-
# GREEN LION,
->Or the<-
## LIGHT of the PHILOSOPHERS.
### To all Lovers of the Hermetic Art. <note>The "Hermetic Art" refers to alchemy, named after Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational alchemical texts were attributed.</note>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop cap 'S' featuring a large capital letter surrounded by scrolling floral and leaf patterns within a square border.</image-desc>

*Most worthy and dear Friends.*

In *The Golden Lion*, <note>original: "Goude Leeuw"</note> page 215, I had promised to write once more and at greater length on the Nature of Salts derived from Metals, Minerals, Vegetables, and Animal matters, and therefore I have now wished to bring this Book to light; in which is recounted the manner in which the Matter must be handled; and for what purpose one has need of the same: as also how the <term>∇ ∇</term> <note>The triangle pointing downward is the alchemical symbol for Water. The double symbol likely refers to "philosophical waters" or potent chemical solvents.</note> are opened, and how one shall prepare them in this Art of the Philosophers, so that they may be used in Medicines.

Many Lovers of this Art have wished to reproach me in the past year, saying that I was too slow in fulfilling my promise: but I must confess, that the desire and love for the Art had almost fallen away from me, due to the heavy Wars and miseries that have plagued our dear Fatherland. <note>This likely refers to the "Rampjaar" (Year of Disaster) in 1672 or the subsequent Franco-Dutch War, which caused significant upheaval in the Netherlands.</note> And this is truly no Art to maintain in times of War, but only in times of Peace: and we know, that, where Pea-

<summary>Goossen van Vreeswyk introduces his new treatise on the "Green Lion," explaining that it fulfills a previous promise to detail the extraction of salts from various substances for medicinal use. He apologizes for the delay, citing the distractions and distress caused by the wars affecting the Dutch Republic.</summary>

<keywords>Green Lion, alchemy, chemical salts, medicinal chemistry, 17th-century warfare, Goossen van Vreeswyk, Hermetic Art, philosophical water</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's preface. The text flows from the previous page's unfinished sentence: "and we know, that, where Pea-" completing the word "Peace" (Dutch: *Vrede*).</meta>

## PREFACE

-ce is, God is found, from whom the **Art** was brought forth. In these times, as all commerce—and the book trade among it—is almost at a standstill, and paper being very costly and still difficult to obtain, this work of mine could not be printed profitably. Yet now that merciful God has frustrated the evil intentions of the enemies, and we have once again obtained a Peace with England, <note>This refers to the Treaty of Westminster (1674), which ended the Third Anglo-Dutch War.</note> and the French are no longer powerful enough to keep the places they had previously occupied, but have mostly withdrawn from our land; <note>Following the "Rampjaar" (Year of Disaster) in 1672, French forces occupied large portions of the Netherlands until their retreat in 1673 and 1674.</note> I have no longer wished to withhold these my experiences from my friends, but for the honor of God and for the promotion of the general welfare, I make them known to all the world. For through this **Art** <note>The "Art" (Dutch: *Konst*) refers to the practice of alchemy and sacred chemistry.</note> all salts are purged and prepared into a good medicine, as much for humans as for metals; but especially for gold and silver, in order to melt them through it.

The minerals of iron-ore are handled by a smelting fire <image-desc>triangle symbol for fire or heat</image-desc> in a furnace with the blowing of bellows, which may then be used for very many things, in peace as well as in war, in the cities, on land, and at sea: for this is the most necessary mineral that God has given to mankind; and from this, all kinds of tools and instruments are made for almost all handicrafts; with it, the land is plowed and cultivated, and the wood in the forests is chopped; with it, heavy ships are built, to [travel] throughout the whole world, through all seas and lands, East and West and over

<summary>The author explains that the publication of this work was delayed by the Anglo-Dutch and Franco-Dutch wars, but is now possible due to the returning peace. He describes the alchemical importance of purifying salts and the civilizational necessity of iron for tools, agriculture, and global maritime trade.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, salt purification, iron-ore, metallurgy, Franco-Dutch War, Treaty of Westminster, 17th-century printing, global trade</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface from the previous page, where the author was discussing the necessity of iron for building ships and exploring the world.</meta>

-># PREFACE<-

to be navigated and visited; indeed, therein is the power of Heaven hidden. For if we had nothing else in the world but Gold and Silver, they could serve us as no guide on the wild sea, as Mars <note>Mars (♂) is the alchemical and astrological symbol for iron.</note> does so well, through the bravery of his nature.

In Mars is the Magnet of all Planets hidden, which constantly lives through its Salt and Fire <note>Fire is represented by the triangle symbol (△).</note>. This shall not be found in others; neither in the dull Mercury, nor the blind Jupiter <note>Jupiter (♃) is the symbol for tin.</note>, nor the crippled Saturn <note>Saturn (♄) is the symbol for lead.</note>, nor the fickle Venus. Under Mars are also included all the Planets of the Salt, which likewise have their Magnet, which one cannot perceive before the Salts are melted or burnt in the Fire, which then draw the Air toward themselves. So it is also when the Mineral of Mars is melted into a Metal or Body <note>original Latin: *Corpus*</note>; then the Iron draws the lodestone <term>lodestone: a naturally magnetized piece of the mineral magnetite</term> toward itself: yet the reason for this you shall not be able to understand, unless you think upon the Spirit of the World <note>original Latin: *Spiritus Mundi*; in alchemical philosophy, this is the "world soul" or vital spirit that permeates all matter and connects the heavens to the earth.</note>; and that shall teach you why the lodestone draws the mineral of iron-ore to itself, and the iron thereafter draws the stone. Iron has its Magnet within itself, so that it can show us the four parts of the world <note>The cardinal directions of the compass: North, South, East, and West.</note>, as has been treated in my previous writings. So it is also with the Salts that have been melted by the fire; for then they visibly draw the Air to themselves in the form of Water: the reason is that they have become Magnetic through the Fire, and this happens not because the Salt is dead, but because of the power of the Fire, which has united itself with that Salt.

<meta>catchword: Venus</meta>

<summary>The author explains the unique magnetic properties of iron (Mars) and its spiritual connection to the Spiritus Mundi, contrasting its utility in navigation with other metals like gold or lead.</summary>
<keywords>Mars, iron, magnetism, lodestone, Spiritus Mundi, alchemical symbols, salt, navigation, four cardinal directions</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface, shifting from the unique magnetic properties of iron (Mars) to the chemical composition and "natures" of other metals and various salts.</meta>

-># PREFACE<-

Venus also has a magnet; but this has a completely different nature than Mars: for Mars <term>Mars (♂): the alchemical symbol for iron</term> has his power from the fire; and Venus <term>Venus (♀): the alchemical symbol for copper</term> has her power from a combustible Sulfur <term>Sulfur (🜍): one of the Three Primes in alchemy, representing soul and inflammability</term> with her Salt, which is also very impure.

Saturn <term>Saturn (♄): the alchemical symbol for lead</term> mostly has the same nature; since his spirit is of a very evil Mercury <term>Mercury (☿): both the metal quicksilver and the Prime representing spirit and fluidity</term>, and his Salt almost burns in the fire <note>Represented by the triangle symbol (△).</note>.

Jupiter <term>Jupiter (♃): the alchemical symbol for tin</term> has much Mercury, with some Sulfur, and very little Salt: therefore it is that the same cracks between the teeth when one bites on it <note>The author refers to the "cry of tin," a distinct crackling sound made when tin is bent or bitten, which alchemists used as a diagnostic test for the metal's purity.</note>; and this happens because its body is of Mercury and Sulfur.

These four—namely, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn—are almost brothers of one nature, so that they can tinge <term>tinge: in alchemy, to "tinge" (tingeren) means to transform or color a metal, often associated with the process of transmutation into gold</term> nothing, but must themselves be tinged: yet he who can heal is greater than he who has need of healing.

Gold and Silver are also distinct, as is described extensively in this book: yet the foremost of all metals is the Sun <term>The Sun (☉): the alchemical symbol for gold</term>, and by Mars the entire world is mastered through his fine power.

The Salts are also distinct from one another, such as Nitre <term>Nitre (🜔): also known as saltpeter, a key ingredient in gunpowder</term> and Common Salt <term>Common Salt (⊝): sodium chloride, often representing the physical "body" or stability of matter in the Tria Prima</term>: among the Mineral Salts are Vitriol <term>Vitriol (🜕): a metallic sulfate, usually of iron or copper</term> and Alum <note>Symbolized by "O" in the original text; a mineral salt used historically in dyeing and medicine.</note>. Those held to be Minerals are Antimony <term>Antimony (♁): a semi-metal often used by alchemists to purify gold</term> and Sulfur. Among the other kingdoms, such as the Vegetable and Animal, are the Salts that come forth from their ashes, which will be treated in the remainder of this book.

This treatise shall then serve the lovers of this glorious Art <note>The "Art" refers to alchemy or hermetic chemistry.</note>, which is a Mother of all Arts; through which all others must flourish, and trade and pros-

<meta>catchword: -perity (from "vaert")</meta>

<summary>The author explains the chemical "natures" of the planets/metals, noting that while iron (Mars) is dominant, others like tin and lead are "impure" and need transformation. It also introduces the classification of salts from the mineral, vegetable, and animal kingdoms.</summary>
<keywords>Venus, Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, Sulfur, Salt, Tinge, Gold, Nitre, Vitriol, Antimony, Alchemical symbols</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface's discussion on the nature of metals, specifically transitioning from the dominance of gold and iron to the foundational role of smelting and salts in human industry and biblical history. The catchword "onver-" from the previous page completes the word "onvervaert" (fearlessly).</meta>

-># PREFACE<-

...fearlessly have their lives among men.

This Art is perfectly the Master; because Gold <term>Gold (☉)</term>, Silver <term>Silver (☽)</term>, Iron <term>Iron (♂)</term>, Copper <term>Copper (♀)</term>, Tin <term>Tin (♃)</term>, Mercury <term>Mercury (☿)</term>, and Lead <term>Lead (♄)</term>, which come forth from the earth, are brought to light through the useful and glorious Art of Smelting, with which one sees trade conducted with great profit: which could not happen without this means, if the Salt and the Fire <note>Represented by the triangle symbol (△).</note> were not governed by this Art, for then all mining works would be lost; namely, without the Art of Smelting, both of Salts and of Metals. Therefore, this Art was called by the Ancient Philosophers the Art of Smelting or *Alkali*, which are the Salts passed through the Fire. For this reason, no one should imagine that this Art would only be an art of the Alchemists; but indeed a Salt- and Smelting-art, just as the Ancients held it to be; and God has also shown us in the example of *Lot's Wife*, who was changed into a pillar of salt; and in the overturning of the cities of *Sodom* and *Gomorrah*, with their entire regions, which were burned by the fire from Heaven and <term>calcined: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to remove volatile substances or to oxidize it, reducing it to powder</term> into dust and ashes, of which there is still a Sea <note>A reference to the Dead Sea.</note> to this very day: and from this the word *Alkali* has come. 

Yet some have mockingly wished to call this *All-bare* <note>original: "Al kael." A Dutch pun on "Alkali." "Kael" means bald, bare, or destitute, implying that the pursuit of alchemy leaves one with nothing.</note>, and have decried this Art as cursed and Devilish, saying that many people have been ruined by it while they sought to obtain large lumps of Gold <term>Gold (☉)</term> from their crucibles and chimneys, which they had as the sole objective of all their labors; but it is entirely

<summary>The author defends the art of smelting and "Alkali" as a foundational science for mining and commerce, distancing it from the "destitute" reputation of greedy alchemists while citing biblical precedents for chemical transformation.</summary>
<keywords>smelting, alkali, salt, calcination, metals, Lot's Wife, Sodom and Gomorrah, alchemical symbols, crucibles, mining</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface's discussion on the chemical and spiritual properties of metals and minerals. It moves from a general defense of the "Art of Smelting" (Alchemy/Chemistry) to a specific breakdown of how planets correspond to metallic natures and the role of various salts in the three kingdoms of nature.</meta>

-># PREFACE<-

Venus <term>Copper (♀)</term> also has a Magnet; but this has an entirely different nature than Mars <term>Iron (♂)</term>: for Mars has its power from the fire; and Venus has her power from a combustible Sulfur <note>Represented by the symbol 🜍.</note> with her Salt, which is also very impure.

Saturn <term>Lead (♄)</term> mostly has the same nature; since its spirit is of a very evil Mercury <term>Quicksilver (☿)</term>, and its Salt is almost burned in the Fire <note>Represented by the triangle symbol (△ or 🜂).</note>.

Jupiter <term>Tin (♃)</term> has much Mercury, with some Sulfur, and very little Salt: that is why it crackles between the teeth when one bites on it; and this happens because its body is made of Mercury and Sulfur.

These four, namely Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn, are almost brothers of the same nature, so that they can tinge <term>tinge: an alchemical term meaning to transform or "dye" a base metal into a higher one</term> nothing, but must themselves be tinged: yet he who can heal is greater than he who stands in need of healing.

Gold <term>Gold (☉)</term> and Silver <term>Silver (☽)</term> are also distinct, as is described at length in this book: but the most noble of all metals is Gold, and by Mars <note>Meaning Iron, the metal of weaponry and tools.</note> the whole world is compelled with its power.

The Salts are also distinct from one another, such as Salt-ammoniac <note>Represented by the symbol 🜔.</note> and Common Salt: among the mineral salts are Alum <note>Represented by the symbol 🜖.</note> and Saltpetre <note>Represented by the symbol O or 🜕.</note>. Those considered minerals are Mercury and Sulfur. Among the other kingdoms, such as the Vegetable and Animal, there are the salts that come forth from their ashes, which will be treated in the remainder of this book.

This treatise shall then serve the lovers of this glorious Art, which is a mother of all arts; through which all others must flourish, and trade and prosperity

It also categorizes various salts and minerals found in the mineral, vegetable, and animal kingdoms.</summary>

...faring among men, have their life.

This Art is perfectly the Master; because Gold <term>Gold (☉)</term>, Silver <term>Silver (☽)</term>, Iron <term>Iron (♂)</term>, Copper <term>Copper (♀)</term>, Tin <term>Tin (♃)</term>, Mercury <term>Quicksilver (☿)</term>, and Lead <term>Lead (♄)</term>, which come forth from the earth, are brought to light through the useful and glorious Art of Smelting, with which one sees commerce conducted with great profit: which could not happen without this means, if Salt and Fire <term>Fire (△)</term> were not governed by this Art, for then all mining works would be lost; namely, without the Art of Smelting, both of Salts and of Metals. Therefore, this Art was called by the Ancient Philosophers the Art of Smelting or *Alkali*, which are the Salts that have passed through the Fire <note>Represented by the triangle symbol △.</note>.

For this reason, no one should imagine that this Art is merely an Art of the Alchemists; but rather a Salt and Smelting Art, just as the Ancients regarded it; and God has also shown us this in the example of *Lot's Wife*, who was changed into a pillar of salt; and in the overturning of the cities of *Sodom* and *Gomorra*, with their entire regions, which were burned by the fire of Heaven and calcined <term>calcined: reduced to a powdery residue by roasting or burning</term> to dust and ashes, of which there is still a Sea to this very day: and from this the word *Alkali* originated.

Yet some have mockingly wished to call this *Al kael* <note>A Dutch pun on 'Alkali'. "Al kael" literally means "all bare" or "entirely empty," suggesting the art leaves its practitioners penniless.</note>, and have decried this Art as cursed and Devilish, saying that many people have been ruined by it, while they sought to obtain large lumps of Gold <term>Gold (☉)</term> from their crucibles and chimneys, which they had as the sole aim of all their labors; but it is entirely

<summary>The author defends the 'Art of Smelting' (alchemy) as a vital science for mining and commerce, linking the term 'Alkali' to the biblical destruction of Sodom and Gomorrah. He dismisses critics who view alchemy only as a fraudulent pursuit of gold.</summary>

-># PREFACE.<-

...ignorance: For this is no Art for false and deceptive—or indeed by vain hope hideously deceived—*Alchemists*; but rather one that was famous among the Ancient Philosophers, and is still held in high honor by the modern-day Sages, when it teaches us to work in Salt and with Gold. For he who rightly knows Salt shall not labor in Gold <term>Gold (☉)</term> alone; but with Salt make a fine beginning and end, according to the lesson of *Basilius* <note>Likely referring to Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist known for his writings on antimony and salts.</note>.

This Art was taught by God to the Children of Adam, as one reads in the Book of *Genesis*, *chapter 4, verse 22, concerning Tubal-cain*, an instructor of every worker in copper and iron: so that this Art is not intended for making Gold, as many imagine, who seek to extract Gold from an incomplete thing—which strives against Nature: for if they take any incomplete thing, no perfect *Tincture* <term>Tincture: in alchemy, a potent substance or "medicine" capable of transmuting or perfecting base matter</term> can come of it; since, in this Art as well, no thing can give what it does not possess. One need only look at the likeness of a grain of wheat, which is indeed perfect in itself, and yet must be helped by others, even more perfect, namely the Rain <term>Water (🜄)</term> and the Salt, which then give fine, perfect powers to the grain. It is just so with the Metals, which must be nourished by Salt-water <term>Salt-water (🜄)</term>, of which enough is described in what follows.

I had not yet intended to break off this treatise, but have now been forced to make an end to my *labors* <note>original: "Labores"</note> here, because I have been appointed as Mining Master <term>Mining Master (Berg-meester): a high-ranking official responsible for overseeing mining operations and metallurgical standards</term> to *Sweden*.

<meta>catchword: to</meta>

In the past year of 1673, I also served as a <term>Mine Master (Berg-meester)</term> <note>A high-ranking official responsible for overseeing mining operations, technical standards, and legal disputes within a mining district.</note> in the <term>Liège region (Luickerland)</term> <note>A sovereign territory in the Holy Roman Empire, now part of modern-day Belgium, famous for its coal and metal mines.</note>, which also hindered my philosophical works and investigations. Consequently, I have been able to share fewer observations with the Art-loving reader; yet I do not doubt that my friends will still favorably and gratefully accept this, my dutiful and affectionate labor.

It is now my intention, if Almighty God spares my life, to describe a second part of my *Cabinet of Minerals*, in which I shall deal with all the minerals, their development and growth within the earth, as well as their nature and the form in which they are brought to light by Nature, along with many other matters pertaining to <term>mining (Berg-werken)</term>. Farewell.

## TO THE HONOR
### O F
# THE AUTHOR
### OF THIS
## T R E A T I S E.

*Let us all listen to Vreeswijk <note>original: "Vreeswik"; referring to Goerdt (Gerrit) Vreeswijk (c. 1625–1689), a Dutch mineralogist and alchemist.</note>, who comes to present to us
The secrets of the true God, who governs the world.
He is going to show us that he can, without boasting,
Extract the treasures from the barren earth;
All that he does surprises the most learned minds.
Alas! Must it be that his Experience <note>In this context, "Experience" refers to experimental practice and hands-on laboratory work.</note>,
In which so few people participate, causes simpletons <note>original: "Nigaux"; refers to fools or those ignorant of the "Great Art" of alchemy.</note>
To speak ill of the effects of his noble science?
Has anyone heard tell of a spirit who can better
Separate every thing, whether similar or contrary?
Would one not say that he must have come from the Heavens,
Considering the knowledge he possesses, both celestial and <term>sublunary</term> <note>Referring to the earthly realm below the moon; in alchemy, this implies mastery over both spiritual/astrological influences and physical matter.</note>.
Let us seek his favor, if we cherish gold;
Let us hear him discourse against <term>Galenism</term> <note>original: "la Galenique"; the traditional medical system based on the four humors. Alchemists often opposed Galenic medicine in favor of chemical or Paracelsian remedies.</note>:
As long as he shares with me his rich treasure,
I shall fear neither Tyrant nor Critic.*

->**B. PIELAT**<-
->*Doctor of Medicine.*<-

->To make Cinnabar from Mercury without the addition of<-
->any other things.<-

Take <term>mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; also known as quicksilver</note> and <term>gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; referred to here as Sol</note> in equal parts. From this, a *water* <note>original Latin: Aqua</note> must be distilled with common <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>. In this <term>antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; in this context, the author uses the symbol for antimony to describe the solvent or the "water" produced in the previous step</note> the mercury shall be dissolved, which then must remain standing in the <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> for 24 hours. Thereafter, draw off a *water* <note>original Latin: Aqua</note> from it 6 or 8 times together, according to one's desire for labor, in order to better open the Matter. When the <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> is then drawn off, the mercury shall remain lying at the bottom of the glass like a lime <note>a "calx" or powdery residue left after heating</note>, which you shall then take out of the glass and grind small in a glass mortar. After that, you shall take just as much mercury again and rub it in until it is no longer seen. This <term>amalgam</term> <note>original: Algamen; a mixture of mercury with another metal</note> must you then lay upon an open flat dish; thus it shall become green in the space of 20 days. Take thereafter this <term>antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> of mercury and place it in a coated glass <term>retort</term> <note>a laboratory vessel used for distillation, usually with a long, downward-pointing neck</note>, driving the same for 24 hours with a great fire. Then you shall find the <term>antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> of mercury as a red <term>cinnabar</term> <note>original: Sinober; a bright red mineral composed of mercury sulfide</note>, of which it is written in no other books how to make it *by itself* <note>original Latin: per se</note>, or through its own power, without any

No one needs to think that I wish to tell dreams here, merely idle imaginings of a weak or sickly head; for dreams are not always dreams, but also indeed Divine and Spiritual inspirations, by which the spirit of man is enlightened, and led and guided in subtle matters beyond the common understanding of natural reason. # The Green Lion,

For he says, that these were already distilled by *Saturn* <note>In alchemical symbolism, Saturn represents lead, but also the process of putrefaction or decay—the "blackening" phase where the material dies to be reborn.</note>, which does not seem to us a false appearance; since it is so light that it could almost be carried away by the wind, like dust and ashes. What then do our *colleagues* <note>Original: "Confraters," meaning fellow practitioners of the alchemical art.</note> say of the *Matter* that is shown to us here? Shall we take it, or not? I shall then venture to call out to him again. Highly respected, great and mighty Lord *Mercury*, hear us humble ones, who heartily pray to you for faithful assistance and good counsel, so that each of us may work with utility in his *Matter*: for without you it is impossible to arrive at that.

->*Alchemists.*<-

First he speaks of Gold, then of Vinegar, and finally of Steel; and this is what has made our purses so empty, as well as the purses of all those who have associated with us. Therefore, do not believe him, no matter how much he may say about the Matter <term>Matter</term> <note>Referring to the *Materia Prima*, the fundamental starting material needed to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, and let us part from him; for, as far as we can tell, he seems to be some departed soul or a wandering spirit seeking rest among us; let us ask him from what body he was last separated. Would you, wise Spirit, be willing to tell us where your dwelling was before this, just before you came here to us?

<meta>catchword: Mer-</meta>

<summary>Mercury concludes his advice by urging the alchemists to follow the teachings of Sendivogius, but the alchemists, embittered by their poverty, suspect Mercury is merely a ghost and demand to know his origins.</summary>

# The Green Lion, / Mercury. 32.

In the dust of the earth, from which you were made; just as all things that are found in the world: but primarily, in Wine, in Water, in the Metals and Minerals, and in Fire. My carriage is the Air, with which I am sent from one place to another: yet no one can recognize me in any other way than through the Steel of the Philosophers <note>The "Steel of the Philosophers" often refers to a specific catalyst or mineral agent—sometimes associated with iron or "the magnet"—used to attract the universal spirit.</note>, and their Wine; if these two are known, then you need seek no others: for the Salt of the Metals is the Steel, and their Wine is their very blood; therefore, let all other species <term>species</term> <note>In this context, "species" refers to the outward forms or specific types of matter that alchemists often fruitlessly experimented with.</note> go, and first seek Nature where God has placed her, namely in the Salt of the Metals and their Mercury <note>original: ☿</note>.

->*Alchemists.*<-

What should we now do with this one? Nothing other than to complain about him before Mars <note>In alchemy, Mars represents Iron and the qualities of strength, heat, and binding.</note>, who still holds him prisoner: therefore let us speak together with one voice. Lord Mars, since you have this spirit here in your control, we pray to you that you would punish him; for without you it is impossible for us to be released with honor from this devil, who makes us almost insane, so that we would have to curse the art <note>The "art" (Latin: *ars*) refers to the Art of Alchemy itself.</note>, whether we like it or not; so we pray, hear us.

<meta>catchword: Mars.</meta>

<summary>Mercury explains that he exists within all elements but can only be captured using the "Steel" and "Wine" of the Sages. The frustrated Alchemists, feeling driven to madness by his riddles, appeal to Mars to restrain and punish the spirit.</summary>

# or the Light of the Philosophers.

->Mars.<-

# The Green Lion,
<sig>B6</sig>
<meta>catchword: Al-</meta>

...has shown the cliff and stone rock.

### *Alchemists.*

Indeed, he thoroughly points us toward a cliff, by which we come to be stranded, bottomless and mastless, and through this Art <note>The "Art" refers to the practice of alchemy.</note> have fallen upon a lee shore <note>original: "leeger Wal"; a nautical term for a dangerous position where the wind blows a ship toward the shore, making it difficult to escape wrecking.</note>.

### *Mars.*

It seems that this is a general plague; therefore I advise you to separate from me in time, and seek another counselor, who shall then lead you so well that you might easily break your neck and legs; and finally he shall further <term>distill</term> <note>Distillation: the process of purifying a liquid by heating it into vapor and then condensing it back into liquid.</note> and <term>sublimate</term> <note>Sublimation: in alchemy, the process of heating a solid substance until it turns into vapor and then collects again as a solid in a purified form.</note> you, so that you may disappear in smoke and ashes; and no man shall be able to know where your rest might be between Heaven and Earth. For as long as you are in the search for the <term>Matter</term> <note>The "Matter" (Materia) refers to the primary substance or "First Matter" required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, you always run from one thing to another, without holding fast to anything. But look, there comes a man who will perhaps please you, for he seems to have a bag full of tricks regarding the <term>Matter</term>: go speak to him, and I shall rest in the meantime, until you might have greater need of me, and then I shall not leave you in distress; but you must shout quite loudly when you wish to wake me from my sleep. Holla! There is a knocking; I am leaving.

or the Light of the Philosophers.

### Alchemists.

There is yet another knock; we must ask who is there, and what his name might be, so that we may know how we should receive him. Now, open the door. My friend, what is your desire, and who are you? Make this known to us, so that it may be announced to the Brothers of the College of Philosophers <note>The "College of Philosophers" refers to a symbolic or literal gathering of alchemists seeking the ultimate truth of nature.</note>.

### Old Man.

I am Saturn <note>In alchemical allegory, Saturn represents the metal Lead. He is often depicted as a sickly, old, or mutilated man, symbolizing the "base" and "dead" state of matter before it is purified.</note>, old in days and gray of hair; my eyes tear up, and my soul and spirit are being pressed out of my limbs; and my entire body is full of watery boils <note>original: "waterbuilen"; likely a reference to the impurities or "dropsy" of the base metal lead.</note>, from which blood and salt emerge, causing me great pain: and because of this I have been spat upon, because I allowed myself to be seduced by man, and have drunk from the wine of wrath. Shall there not now be someone who can heal me of my defects, so that I may become fit to enter among you, once you gain good knowledge of who I am?

### Alchemists.

Yes, we see well enough what kind of bird you are—entirely without feathers, but not without sores <note>The "featherless bird" is a common alchemical symbol for "fixed" matter that cannot fly away (vaporize) during heating.</note>: yet we pity you for the weakened old age of your limbs; therefore we wish to deliberate on by what means one might heal you.

# The Green Lion,

->*Old Man.*<-

Oh! Help me then, that I may be healed of my boils and pains, which cast me down entirely.

->*Alchemists.*<-

Brothers, we have had a proverb among us that an old and crippled man was no longer of use to the world; therefore, let us help this one, so that he may reach the place where we have brought many, vanishing into wind and smoke <note>This "vanishing into wind and smoke" refers to the common failure of alchemists whose materials evaporated away without producing the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. Merrily then to the labor; first pull off his skin, and after that the blood; then one shall <term>calcine</term> <note>Calcine: to reduce a substance to powder or ash through intense heat.</note> him to dust and ashes: these ashes shall be strewn upon the water. Afterward, one shall draw his fine bones out of it again, which are seasoned with <term>Salt</term> <note>In alchemy, Salt represents the "body" or the solid, fixed principle of a substance.</note>; and after that, we shall boil our pot with them, and use his fine blood as <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> against the destruction of the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>. This was taught to us by <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; the central messenger and transformative agent in alchemy.</note>, who had brought the warlike <term>Mars</term> <note>Mars represents the metal Iron.</note> as a prisoner before the King. If we now wish to perform good work, let us take <term>Mercury</term> and <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍; Sulfur represents the "soul" or the active, combustible principle.</note>, and this shall be the final resolution of our business.

->*Old Man.*<-

Alas, wretched me! How have I fallen so unhappily into these disastrous hands? Shall nothing good then come forth from my death, which I must suffer so sadly?

<summary>The Alchemists resolve to "heal" the Old Man (Saturn/Lead) through a violent process of calcination and separation, hoping to extract salt and use his "blood" to balance the elements of fire and water. The Old Man laments his fate, fearing his suffering will be purposeless.</summary>

# or the Light of the Philosophers.

But help me then quickly out of this sorrow, into which the cursed Dragon <note>In alchemy, the Dragon often represents volatile matter, or a corrosive acid (like "philosopher's vinegar") that dissolves the metal.</note> has brought me with its evil drink; just as the cunning Serpent, which had hung itself around my tree. And if you cannot kill me, then my body shall not rise again. The Birds of the Sea <note>Likely referring to the volatile spirits or vapors that rise during distillation and "peck" at the fixed matter.</note> have pecked out my eyes; they are red as blood, and they bring them then into their nest, with which they nourish their young; and my body becomes earth, in which the food of those young is found.

->*Alchemists.*<-

Let us part from this Old One; for he shall soon make his Testament <note>A "Testament" in alchemy refers to the final instructions or the revealed secret of a process before the material "dies" or transforms.</note>: but tell us first from whence you have come, who is your Father, and what Mother brought you forth?

->*Old Man.*<-

The Sun is my Father, and I am its Mother: that is to say, when I was <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, they took me for the Mother; but now that I have received a body, they take me for the Husband, or the Red Servant of Bernardus <note>Referring to Bernard of Trevisan, a 15th-century alchemist. The "Red Servant" is an allegorical figure representing a specific stage of the "Great Work" (Rubedo) where the matter becomes fixed and red.</note>, or the Gold of the Ancient Sages.

->*Alchemists.*<-

These reasons are impossible for us to understand; you godless rogue, you seek to deceive us: but no, we shall [do something to] your body...

<summary>The Old Man describes his suffering and symbolic death at the hands of the "Dragon" and "Birds of the Sea," while the Alchemists question his origins, revealing his dual nature as both Mercury and the "Red Servant" of alchemical tradition.</summary>

# or the Light of the Philosophers.

This same fate shall likely also meet me, if I do not wish to stop such foolishness in time; for I find in truth that an **Alchemist** among many decent people is viewed exactly as a scabby sheep; and that a drunkard, whoremonger, gambler, and the like, enjoy more honor today than an **Alchemist**; since everyone is terrified of those beastly **Alchemists** who want to make **Gold**, which belongs to God alone, about which more shall be treated at a more suitable place in the following.

These words do not concern sensible, wise, and virtuous **Chemists** or **Philosophers**; but only those who seek to make **Gold** out of certain substances, such as from <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note>, <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: $\unicode{x263F}$</note>, and <term>Salt</term> <note>original symbol: $\ominus$</note>, which strives against Nature and is entirely vain. # or the Light of the Philosophers.

-tion to show to the Friends of the same; since from the same all wisdom proceeds, by the will of God, who is our Teacher.

In the work of the Ancient Philosophers they say that their **Green Lion** is a **Salt of the Metals**, produced by the <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note> of the Wise; and this is nothing other than a volatile air, with a salty spirit of the Metals, which is made out of the same—indeed, with the Metals—as far as I have been able to understand from my own experiments.

The **Salt of the Ancient Wise** proceeds from the Metals: they also call this same thing a <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: △</note>, a <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>, and by many more other names; yet it matters little by what word it is called, so long as one may know it and knows how to obtain it. I call it the **Spirit of the World**, which is brought to light by the Art, with bile and vinegar, or with <term>Salt</term> <note>symbol: ⊖</note> and <term>Vinegar</term> <note>symbol: ♒</note> of <term>Sal-Ammoniac</term> <note>symbol: ◻; a mineral salt often used as a reagent</note>: yet it must be well noted that the Philosophers sometimes substitute <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: "Wijnsteen"</note> for Vinegar, and <term>Tartar</term> <note>symbol: ☓</note> for <term>Vitriol</term> <note>symbol: 🜊; a sulfate mineral</note>. They often use "bile" to mean <term>Sulfur</term> <note>symbol: 🜖</note>, and <term>Theta</term> <note>the Greek letter θ, used here as a shorthand symbol</note> for <term>Copper</term> <note>symbol: ♀; also the sign for the planet Venus</note>: but he who is concerned with this may do as I have done and investigate it himself, so it shall become known to him what is red and what is white.

We say further, that in <term>Saturn</term> <note>symbol: ♄; usually representing the metal Lead</note> the spirit of—

# The Green Lion,

Mercury resides, who is the Key of the Art; for although I do not yet know how to obtain the **Philosopher's Stone**, it is nonetheless permitted for me to write about what I have seen in Nature or produced through the Art by my own labor; such as a **Red Lake** or **Sea** out of the Metals, with the help of the spirit of <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note> of the Ancient Sages.

This <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note> is a Mother of all things in the whole world, who gives life to everything: her rest is in the Metals; the wagons or horses are the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> and the **Moon** <note>Silver</note>, which are carried by this <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>; yet whoever wishes to seek the same, let him first learn to know all the **Waters of Nature**, which are many: such as Rain <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, River <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, Snow <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, Ice <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, Dew <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, indeed Urine or Piss, Wine, Vinegar, Buttermilk, etc. and for this reason it is called a **Virgin's milk**, because at certain times, it comes forth from the horns of a young Calf. <note>This is an alchemical metaphor; the "horns of a calf" likely refers to the sign of Taurus or a specific shape of a distillation vessel.</note> Some Sages also write that one should take the brains of an **old black Man**, who has hung in the Chimney for seven days, or the blood of a young person of 10 years. <note>These macabre descriptions are allegorical: the "old black man" refers to lead or the "blackness" (nigredo) stage of matter, and the "chimney" refers to the furnace.</note> With this they teach that one must make a <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, one red and the other white: yet this is nothing other than the **Red Servant** of *Bernardus* <note>Referring to Bernard Trevisan, a 15th-century alchemist</note>, which *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine</note> says is the **Magnet of the Sages**. *Sendivogius* calls it his **Steel**. Some-

<summary>The author describes Mercury as the "Key of the Art" and the universal mother, explaining that various natural liquids and macabre allegories (like the 'old black man') are actually coded instructions for creating alchemical solvents.</summary>

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

Some call it <term>Antimony</term> <note>symbol: ♁; in this context, it refers to the primal earth or the metallic ore rather than just the modern element</note>, as one can read in the *Concordance* page 98. For others it is <term>Saturn</term> <note>symbol: ♄; Lead</note>, or <term>Jupiter</term> <note>symbol: ♃; Tin</note>, and furthermore the spirit out of <term>Mars</term> <note>symbol: ♂; Iron</note> and <term>Venus</term> <note>symbol: ♀; Copper</note>. I would consider this the right way to go astray; for there is but one <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: 🜄</note> through which the **Gold of the Sages** is dissolved, and this they also call <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>, and others <term>Saturn</term> <note>symbol: ♄</note>, indeed the *Green Lion*, a *Virgin's milk*, a *Vinegar*, a spirit from the Metals, a <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, and <term>Libra</term> <note>symbol: ♎; likely referring to the weight or the 'balance' of the salts</note> of Urine, a spirit from Rain <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: 🜄</note>: but I call it a spirit that comes above the earth through Nature with the help of the Sun. This spirit is the **Vitriol of the Sages**, of which one can read in the *Golden Lion*, where I have treated the same at great length, as well as in the *Red Lion*. <note>The "Golden Lion" and "Red Lion" are likely titles of other alchemical works by the same author or part of the same collection.</note>

This Water I write of, the Ancients also called a *Dragon*, an old Serpent, a <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: 🜂; alchemists often called their corrosive solvents "fire" because they "burned" and transformed metals</note>, indeed the spirit of <term>Saturn</term> <note>symbol: ♄</note>, as well as the soul of the Metals, and the blood of the Earth. # The Green Lion,

called <term>Saturn</term> <note>Original symbol: ♄</note> and <term>Star</term> <note>Original symbol: ∗; likely referring to the "Starry Regulus" of antimony or a specific salt</note>; yet not the common ones, but those that are made through these, and brought forth by the art of the <term>Steel of the Ancient Sages</term> <note>A philosophical catalyst or purified metal used to "attract" the spirit of the world</note>. This is also a Magnet of *Basilius* <note>Basilius Valentinus, a famous but perhaps apocryphal 15th-century alchemist</note>, being a red earth, in which <term>Fire</term> <note>Original symbol: Δ</note>, and rays of the Sun and Moon are found. This shall be the sign, by which you shall know these: in the separation you shall see the <term>Fire</term> <note>Original symbol: Δ</note> upon the earth, which shall be as red as a Ruby, transparent in its luster. When this has rested on the earth for six days, then one sees **Adam** <note>Symbolizing the "Prime Matter" or the unrefined state of the soul/metal</note> standing in his Water-bath, of which *Basilius* speaks in his rhymes following the 12 Keys <note>Refers to the influential book "The Twelve Keys of Basil Valentine"</note>, saying, *that a Stone is found, from which one causes a volatile Fire <note>Original symbol: Δ</note> to come forth, and which is brought together from white and red.* This one is had in the Month of March, and its death is in October <note>Alchemy often followed the zodiac; March (Aries) represents the start of the work, and October (Scorpio/Libra) represents the stage of "death" or putrefaction</note>. Here everything is revealed that one must have, both regarding the time, and also the Matter: for these names make the Matter, and are the Matter: yet Mars rules the world through the sword and <term>Fire</term> <note>Original symbol: Δ</note>, so that much blood is shed.

# The Green Lion,

We turn then again to the Medicines, which can still be of some service to promote the health of men.

### A Medicine from <term>Nitre</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜔; here representing nitre or saltpeter, often used as a flux in these reactions</note>, <term>Sulfur</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜍</note>, and <term>Antimony</term> <note>Original symbol: ♁</note> against Lung-sickness. <note>original: *Longer-zucht*; a historical term for respiratory ailments such as consumption or tuberculosis</note>

Take: <term>Nitre</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜔</note>, <term>Sulfur</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜍</note>, and <term>Antimony</term> <note>Original symbol: ♁</note>, *of each an equal part*. <note>original: *ana*; a standard medical abbreviation derived from Greek meaning "in equal parts"</note> These must be crushed small, and then placed in an instrument of earth or iron, such as a <term>retort</term> <note>A flask with a long, curved neck used for distilling substances</note>, in which there must be a small hole at the top, large enough for a finger to fit, through which the material is introduced; this is then stopped up with a plug of iron or stone, so that the <term>spirit</term> <note>The volatile gaseous essence released during heating</note> does not fly out. This instrument shall be placed in a furnace, with a moderate fire beneath it, neither too hot nor too cold, so that the material may begin to ignite. Afterward, one shall attach a large <term>receiver</term> <note>original: *Voor-legger*; a glass vessel used to catch and condense the distilled vapors</note> to the retort, well <term>luted</term> <note>original: *geluteert*; sealed airtight with a specialized clay or paste</note>, so that the spirits do not escape through the joints. When this has been so prepared, then one introduces the material in small portions; the substance will then ignite, which with great force shall carry over its spirit and volatile *Body* <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, even as a red mist. This shall be done until all of your material is distilled. Let the instrument then become cold, and take the <term>dead head</term> <note>original: *Caput mortuum*; the solid residue remaining in the retort after distillation</note>

### How One May Obtain an Astralic Salt.

Take <term>Lead</term> <note>Original symbol: ♄</note> and <term>Tin</term> <note>Original symbol: ♃</note>, of each an equal amount <note>original: *ana*; a medical instruction from the Greek 'ana' meaning to use equal parts of each ingredient</note>. Melt these together first in a <term>blast-furnace</term> <note>original: *Wind-oven*; a furnace designed to reach high temperatures by using a bellows or natural draft</note>, using a crucible such as goldsmiths use. Once it is melted, pour it out and let it grow cold. Then take this mass and weigh how heavy it is. Next, take <term>Iron</term> <note>Original symbol: ♂</note> and <term>Copper</term> <note>Original symbol: ♀</note>, an equal amount of each, and add these to the lead and tin: but the iron and copper together must not weigh more than the <term>Tin</term> <note>Original symbol: ♃</note> and <term>Lead</term> <note>Original symbol: ♄</note> which you previously melted together. Then take these metals and <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solveert*</note> them in good <term>Water</term> <note>Original symbol: ∇; in this context, the symbol for water often refers to a strong chemical solvent or acid like *Aqua Fortis*</note>; it will then begin to effervesce and react violently with one another, so much so that it would run out of the glass vessel if you put too much in it: therefore, proceed cautiously and do not add too much

### *A Melting-powder that I use on the Minerals, When Mars and Venus are together, in which one would trust Gold to be.*

<term>Take</term> <note>original: ℞</note> one pound of Mineral, 2 pounds of <term>Lead</term> <note>Original symbol: ♄; Saturn</note>, 2 pounds / of <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜔</note>. These all together pounded small, and / put together, set on fire, and brought into the / Melting-furnace. Note Well: The Mineral must / first be calcined <note>original: *geglooeit*; roasted or heated until glowing to remove impurities</note> in the <term>Fire</term> <note>Original symbol: △</note>, and thereafter washed / off, as is customary. Then the <term>Lead</term> <note>Original symbol: ♄</note> and / <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜔</note> added to it, and melted in the Furnace as before, / which melted Mass is poured out / into a casting-cone <note>original: *giet-poekel*; a cone-shaped mold where heavy metals settle at the bottom</note> which must be / smeared with Wax. Thereafter the Regulus <note>original: *Konink*; literally "king," the button of pure metal found at the bottom of the slag after cooling</note> shall / be struck off from the Slag, which one shall then let / pass off with 4 to 6 parts <term>Lead</term> <note>Original symbol: ♄</note> upon the Cupel <note>original: *Capelle*; a shallow, porous vessel used in assaying to separate precious metals from lead</note>, / according to common usage; which one shall then / separate by means of <term>Water</term> <note>Original symbol: ▽; here referring to *Aqua Fortis* or Nitric Acid</note>, thus one obtains the Gold / and Silver separately, for as much as was in that / Mineral, after which one can make a fine / calculation. This is the shortest way to / test the wild <term>Mars</term> <note>Iron</note> and <term>Venus</term> <note>Copper</note>, how much a <term>Quintal</term> <note>original: *Centener*; a unit of weight roughly equal to 100 pounds</note> / of Ore <note>original: *Meendera*; likely a variation of *mijn-erts*</note> contains; after which one can then / make a calculation, before one starts / to set that up on a large scale.

### To separate a Regulus of <term>Iron</term> <note>Original symbol: ♂; Mars</note> and <term>Antimony</term> <note>Original symbol: ♁</note>.

<term>Take</term> <note>original: ℞</note> <term>Antimony</term> <note>Original symbol: ♁</note>. Let the <term>Iron</term> first become glowing hot in a crucible, and then add the <term>Antimony</term> into it. When it is cold, strike it into pieces; then you shall have the <term>King</term> <note>original: *Konink*; the pure metal button found beneath the slag</note> at the bottom of the crucible if you break it apart. Note Well: You could otherwise pour your molten material into a <term>casting-cone</term> <note>original: *giet-poekel*; a cone-shaped mold used to separate heavy metals from lighter slag</note>, as was taught before. One can also test the ore with <term>Lead</term> <note>Original symbol: ♄; Saturn</note> according to the teachings of <term>Ercker</term> <note>referring to Lazarus Ercker, a renowned 16th-century metallurgist</note>, taking one part of Mineral against 6 to 8 parts of <term>Lead</term>, about which plenty can be read in his work.

Through <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜔</note> and <term>Antimony</term> <note>Original symbol: ♁</note> with <term>Antimony</term>, a *Regulus* is also made. Through <term>Saltpeter</term> and <term>Copper</term> <note>Original symbol: ♀; Venus</note> the same is also accomplished, for those who have good handling in the <term>Fire</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜂</note>.

### Of the Nature of Metals.

All Metals, before they have been in the smelting Fire <term>original symbol: Δ</term>, are called a Stone, and yet they are no Stone; for the reason that it is nothing but a mixed Mass of Sulfur <term>original symbol: 🜍</term>, Salt <term>original symbol: 🜔</term>, and Mercury <term>original symbol: ☿</term>. These three are always in one Mass, as well in the Metal that is molten as that which is still unmolten; and this is called an ore, which is to say, an earth that contains many species <note>In this context, "species" refers to different types or forms of matter</note> within itself, just as was indicated here before. For various species come forth from the earth, as one can see in the Stone called Ore, from which many things can be brought forth; such as Glass, Metal, Sulfur <term>original symbol: 🜍</term> and Salt <term>original symbol: 🜔</term>, even Lime and Ashes, which are all hidden in this Stone. And the Mine-master <note>A "Berg-meester" was a high-ranking official or expert overseer of mining operations</note> calls it an Ore or Mineral. Firstly, the Mountains are opened through the Art <note>Here, "Art" may refer to both the technical skill of mining and the alchemical understanding of the earth's riches</note>; thereafter the Stones are brought out from them, which are called Ore, which are then smelted into Metals; of which *Job* treats in his 28th Chapter at the beginning.

The Moon is compared to silver because it is cold by nature; yet not like Lead <term>original symbol: ♄</term>, but exactly between Gold <term>original symbol: ☉</term> and Lead <term>original symbol: ♄</term>. For if you dissolve Lead <term>original symbol: ♄</term> in Water <term>original symbol: ▽</term>, you will not easily bring the fine calx <note>The powder or "lime" that remains after a metal has been burned or oxidized</note> back to a Metal; the reason is that Lead <term>original symbol: ♄</term> has too much earthiness and Water <term>original symbol: ▽</term> within it, which mostly pass away in smoke, the remainder of which melts into a glass, so that not much good is to be expected from it, other than mainly smoke and stench. ### A Salt from Lead, Tin, and Antimony.

**Recipe.** Lead <term>original symbol: ♄</term>, Tin <term>original symbol: ♃</term>, Antimony <term>original symbol: ♁</term>, *equal parts* <note>original: *ana*, a medical and alchemical term meaning equal parts of each ingredient</note>. The Lead and Tin must first be melted together in a crucible; thereafter one must also add the Antimony into it, and thus it shall melt into a black glass, which easily occurs in a small fire <term>original symbol: △</term>. When this mass has thus melted, then place another melting-crucible over it, which fits tightly upon it; then your Salt shall sublime <note>Sublimation is the process where a solid turns into vapor and then back into a solid on a cooler surface, used here to purify the substance</note> up into the top of the crucible, very beautiful and white as snow, which you can take out when the matter has become cold. Then place your crucibles back into the fire, and work as before, until all the matter has been driven upward. 

Note: You must not provide a great fire, or no white Salt will come forth: the matter also consumes itself entirely during sublimation into a yellow glass on the crucibles in the fire; so that you would suffer loss in the work through this. I share this with you as a wonderful gift; if you now have the desire to obtain such a Salt, then put it into practice, if you are a Master of the fire <note>A "Master of the fire" refers to an alchemist skilled in regulating heat (the "degrees of fire"), which was the most difficult part of the Art</note>; otherwise, it shall go for you as has happened to many others: and he who knows how to prepare this himself will also well know how to find its use; therefore I will write no more extensively of this.

# *Concerning the Materials that must be prepared for the Cupel,*
## *to purify <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term> and <term>Silver</term> <term>☽</term>.*

<note>original: ℞</note> Recipe: Take old bones that have lain in the air for some years, in the open field, in rain and wind. These you shall burn into a lime, which shall be clean and white: then you shall pound them into a powder, and sift them through a sieve. This shall then be boiled in sweet <term>Water</term> <term>∇</term> <note>original symbol: ≈; shorthand for water or fluid</note> until all the salt has been removed. In the event that the <term>Water</term> <term>∇</term> still tastes salty, one shall pour it off and pour fresh water on again. This must be boiled again until all salty taste has vanished. After the material has been dried, the bone-ash is ready, from which you shall make your <term>Cupel</term> <note>A shallow, porous bowl used in assaying to separate precious metals from impurities like lead</note> to purify <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term> and <term>Silver</term> <term>☽</term> upon, and to wash them clean by means of <term>Lead</term> <term>♄</term>: however, this has also been taught in my previous writings.

Out of necessity, one can also make fine <term>Cupels</term> from wood-ash; however, you must leach the salt out of it, just as was stated here regarding the bone-ash. <note>original: NB</note> Note: You must know that these are very liable to crack in the <term>Fire</term> <term>∆</term> and break into pieces, which then causes loss.

The purest separation that can be performed on <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term> occurs by means of <term>Lead</term> <term>♄</term>, when one allows the <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term> to <insert>pass</insert> <note>the catchword below completes this word</note> through the <term>Lead</term> <term>♄</term> three times;

### *Of the Materials which must be prepared for the Cupel, to purify <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term> and <term>Silver</term> <term>☽</term>.*

Take old bones that have lain in the air for some years, in the open field, in rain and wind. These you shall burn until they become a <term>lime</term> <note>original: "kalk"; here referring to the white, powdery residue of calcination</note>, which shall be clean and white: then you shall stamp them into a powder, and bolt the same through a sieve. This shall then be boiled in sweet <term>Water</term> <term>∇</term>, until all the salt is out of it. Should the <term>Water</term> <term>∇</term> still be salty, one shall pour it off and pour on fresh water again. This must then be boiled again, until all salty taste is gone. After the matter is dried, the <term>bone-ash</term> <note>original: "been-aſſchen"</note> is ready, from which you shall make your <term>Cupel</term> <note>original: "Capelle"; a shallow, porous vessel used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals like lead</note>, to purify gold and <term>Silver</term> <term>☽</term> upon it, and to wash them off with <term>Lead</term> <term>♄</term>: however, this is also taught in my previous writings.

In case of necessity, one can also make fine <term>Cupels</term> from wood-ash; but you must leach the salt out of them, just as was said here for the bone-ash. NB. You must know that these very easily come to burst in the <term>Fire</term> <term>Δ</term>, and break into pieces; whereby damage is done.

The purest separation that one can perform on <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term> occurs through <term>Antimony</term> <term>♁</term>, when one lets the <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term> [pass] three times through the <term>Antimony</term> <term>♁</term>

When one separates it by <term>Aqua Regia</term> <term>🜖</term> <note>a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids capable of dissolving gold</note>, there is usually a volatile <term>Copper</term> <term>♀</term> or <term>Sulfur</term> <term>⊕</term> <note>the symbol ⊕ here likely refers to vitriol or sulfurous impurities</note> which then unites with the <term>Gold</term> <term>☉</term>, so that it is not pure: but whoever wishes to know more of that can read it in *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note>.

# The Green Lion,

The Salt of the Metals is called the Gold: without that, say the Philosophers, nothing can be accomplished in the Art <note>The "Art" or "Great Work" refers to the practice of alchemy</note>. The Metals all have one root from which they have been brought forth; namely, from the Salt, and that is also the Gold of the Ancient Sages. Minerals are not among the perfection of the Metals, although one could use them to make such a Salt that could serve the Art. Minerals are for the most part Sulfur <term>🜍</term> and Mercury <term>☿</term>, with very little Salt; which will be discovered when one calcines <note>Heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to ash or powder</note> them by the Fire <term>Δ</term>; for then their entire body <note>original: *Corpus*</note> rises up in a white or red smoke, and they leave a little earth on the bottom, in which is a small portion of Salt, which does not yet have the nature of the Salt of the Metals: for it is the Metals, as has been said before, from which the entire Philosophy first took its origin.

This reasoning will perhaps seem strange to some, because they read in the Philosophical writings of Saturn <term>♄</term> <note>Lead</note>, Jupiter <term>♃</term> <note>Tin</note>, Mercury <term>☿</term> <note>Quicksilver</note>, and Mars <term>♂</term> <note>Iron</note>, Venus <term>♀</term> <note>Copper</note>, and Gold <term>☉</term>; yet by these symbols they have understood the colors that appear in the materials. Saturn <term>♄</term> indeed gives a Salt, but it has the nature of Mars <term>♂</term>; and therefore it can give no perfect Salt of Metals <note>original: *Sal Metallorum*</note>: for that Salt, of which the Ancients [wrote...] <note>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword indicates the next page begins with "writing"</note>

<summary>This page explains that the true "Gold" of the philosophers is actually a "Salt" derived from metals, distinguishing perfect metals from minerals which are too volatile. It also clarifies that planetary symbols in alchemical texts often refer to the sequence of colors observed during the chemical process rather than just the literal metals.</summary>

The Saturn <term>♄</term> of the Philosophers lives in a very strong house, that is surrounded by many bands, from which two fountains come forth: the one of blood, and the other of milk, which they call their Virgin's tears <note>original: *Jonkvrouwens tranen*; this likely refers to a specific chemical distillate or solvent</note>, and has come forth from Adam <note>In alchemy, Adam often represents the "Red Earth" or the primary material of the Great Work</note>.

The Green Lion,

### *To separate Gold very easily from Silver.*

When Gold <term>☉</term> and Silver <term>☽</term> are in a single mass, and the Gold <term>☉</term> is the primary component therein, one must first melt that mass and afterwards pour it into water <term>▽</term>; thus the metal shatters into grit or very small parts, which one shall then dissolve in an *Aqua* <term>℞</term> <note>The symbol ℞ here refers to a solvent, likely *Aqua Fortis* (nitric acid), which was standard for parting silver from gold.</note>. After that, one shall take Mercury <term>☿</term>, which has first been dissolved in water <term>▽</term>, and put it into the *Aqua* <term>℞</term>; then all the Gold <term>☉</term> falls out of the *Aqua* <term>℞</term>, and the Mercury <term>☿</term> remains within it. This liquid should then be poured into a lye made of burnt Salt <term>□</term> <note>The symbol □ represents Salt or Tartar; "burnt salt" usually refers to potash or Salt of Tartar used as an alkali.</note> or wood-ashes: this precipitates the Mercury <term>☿</term> out of both the waters <term>≈</term> <note>original: *beide de ≈*; referring to the combined liquid solutions.</note> to the bottom. NB. Before you put the Mercury <term>☿</term> into the *Aqua* <term>℞</term>, it shall first be washed somewhat with common fresh water <term>▽</term>; then the Mercury <term>☿</term> will precipitate the Gold all the better out of the *Aqua*; which one shall then wash with fresh water <term>≈</term> and dry. Afterwards, one shall melt that with Borax: and if one does not wish to do that, one can melt 4 or 5 parts Lead <term>♄</term> upon a cupel <term>Capelle</term> <note>A cupel is a porous ceramic bowl used in the process of cupellation to refine precious metals.</note>, and then carry the gold-calx <term>Goud-kalk</term> <note>A powder (calx) produced by the chemical treatment of gold.</note> into it, and let it pass off in the common way; thus the Gold is not only brought into a body <term>Corpus</term>, but at the same time is also purified of all the impurity that still remained upon it from the Mercury <term>☿</term> and the *Aqua* <term>℞</term>. NB. One can precipitate Gold through Silver <term>☽</term> in this way, just as Silver is [done] <margin>done</margin> through Copper <term>♀</term>.

Cabinet of Minerals I promised in this Book
to explain.

There are 4 Metals, which in Holy Scripture
are presented to us, as can be read in

Saltpeter, Common Salt <note>original: "Sal Commun"</note>, Sulphur, and Wine-vinegar,
God has caused to be recorded by his Pro-

now tell us, what the first species was,
from which God made the Gold <term>☉</term>,
or from which the Silver <term>☽</term> came forth, from what

that their spirit and soul
would bring forth so much power, as to make a
Tincture from them. <note>In alchemy, a "Tincture" is a potent substance capable of transmuting "base" metals into gold. The author argues that lead, tin, and mercury are "sick" metals that must be transformed, rather than the agents of transformation themselves.</note>

Who is greater, he who can tint <note>tingeren: the alchemical act of transmuting or coloring a metal to change its nature</note>, or he who
shall be tinted? I say then, that he who can
tint is a King. *Luna* <note>The Moon or Silver</note> has no power
to give anything; for she herself has
need of being helped by the King, who
has become rich through his Subjects. Mars <term>♂</term>
cannot exist without Venus <term>♀</term>, who
was first made from his rib, when his soul was
taken from him by the Cross <term>†</term> <note>The cross symbol in this context likely refers to the crucible or the alchemical process of "mortification," where a metal is broken down to its base essence before being reborn. ### or the Light of the Philosophers.

gives, just as *Pythagoras* wrote; which soul is the pure Mantle of the King, with which the Princes of the Army shall be clothed. The Mantle is something other than the King. The King is made of Gold <term>☉</term>; but the Mantle is made by Fire <term>△</term>, which *Basilius* <note>Referring to Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential.</note> calls his Magnet. They say that their Lead <term>♄</term> is also Antimony <term>♁</term> <note>In alchemy, the symbol ♁ often represents the Earth or the "Regulus of Antimony," a crystalline metal used in purifying gold.</note>, indeed Iron <term>♂</term> and Copper <term>♀</term>, with Gold <term>☉</term>, and Fire <term>△</term>, and Salt: but whatever the truth of that may be, I leave to the judgment of those who are greater Masters than I.

The alchemists of today, who give honor to Lead <term>♄</term>, say that it is the First Matter <note>The *Materia Prima*, the original, chaotic substance required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> of all minerals; which I cannot find to be true, since the First Matter of minerals is their Salt; and therefore the Body <note>original: *Corpus*</note> of Lead <term>♄</term> cannot be the First Matter: for the Salt <term>🜔</term> of Lead <term>♄</term> is cold, and the Salt <term>🜔</term> of the Wise is a Fire <term>△</term>, which is their matter. The Salt of Lead <term>♄</term> melts very easily, both in the air and in common fire <term>△</term>; but if its Water <term>▽</term> is taken away from it, then it does not melt. It is the same with Tin <term>♃</term>, and all the other metals.

### To make a beautiful Calx of ♄ <term>Saturn (Lead)</term>.

℞ <note>original: "Recipe" (Take)</note> 1 pound of ♄ <term>Saturn (Lead)</term>, beaten as thin as the back of a knife. This shall be hung above good wine vinegar in a glass; thereafter it must be covered, or tied shut with a bladder. This must then stand in a warm place for 12 or 14 days, and thus a beautiful calx <note>Calx: a powdery residue (here, lead acetate or "Sugar of Lead") produced by the chemical action of vinegar vapors on lead plates.</note> like snow will hang upon this lead plate: you must then wipe this off with a feather, and put the pieces of ♄ <term>Saturn</term> back into the glass as before, and afterwards wipe it off again, until at last you have enough snow, which shall be as sweet as sugar and as bright of luster as crystal, yet not transparent, nor so hard. From this, one can distill an oil, which is a wonderful medicine against the ailments that fall under the Planet of ♄ <term>Saturn</term>. <note>In medical astrology, Saturn was thought to govern the bones, spleen, and "cold" diseases.</note> For although it may be sweet, it is nonetheless cold by nature: and if a pregnant woman were given this oil, it could kill the fruit within her; likewise, if a man or woman were to take some of it for several days, they would become infertile. Therefore, everyone must be warned to handle this medicine with caution, even though it is a noble calx, and much better than that made in the common manner,
