# ...or the Light of the Philosophers.

...can make; but one must not reveal all mysteries. I say only that the entire art lies in the Metals and Minerals; which Metals are turned into dust and ashes by the Minerals, and thereafter dissolved by the 🜔 <term>Salt</term> of the Ancient Sages, which they call Sal Armoniac <note>original: "Sal Armoniac". Known today as Sal Ammoniac or ammonium chloride, it was a vital chemical used to "open" or dissolve metals.</note>, which is their ☿ <term>Mercury</term>, or indeed ♄ <term>Saturn</term> <note>Alchemical symbol for Lead.</note>, as well as 🜍 <term>Sulfur</term> and △ <term>Fire</term>. They also call it by the name of ☉ <term>Gold</term>, Salt of 🜓 <term>Vitriol</term>, and many other names; as also a poisonous Dragon, and a Virgin born from Venus <note>In alchemy, Venus represents Copper.</note>: yet this is nothing other than their Salt, which I named the 🜔 <term>Salt</term> of the earth in *The Golden Lion*: if you understand this, then you know the Green Lion, from which this book takes its name.

Have not all Metals and Minerals come forth from 🜔 <term>Salt</term>, 🜍 <term>Sulfur</term>, and ☿ <term>Mercury</term>? Understand these to be the [essences] of the Sages, and not the common substances. For this shall be found when the Metals and Minerals are burnt to dust and ashes; and then one obtains a Salt, which is brought to light through the Art of Wisdom.

The highest Art is that one can open the Metals without the violence of △ <term>Fire</term>: for then one retains not only their Salt, but also the 🜍 <term>Sulfur</term> and the ☿ <term>Mercury</term>; through which the ☿ <term>Mercury</term> of the Sages is then produced. I write here according to the common reasoning of the Philosophers,

### *From ☿ Sublimate of Mercury, and the Mineral of ♁ Antimony, a White Spirit is made.*

℞ <note>The symbol ℞ is an abbreviation for the Latin *Recipe*, meaning "Take."</note> 1 pound of ♁ <term>Antimony</term> <note>Alchemical symbol for Antimony, a brittle, silvery-gray metalloid used in purifying gold.</note>. Crush it finely. Add to it a quarter [pound] of 🜔 <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>Alchemical symbol for Nitre or Saltpeter, a key oxidizing agent in laboratory work.</note>. Rub these together; and afterwards let it be puffed away <note>The Dutch "verpuft" refers to a sudden vaporization or decrepitation when heated.</note>, or let it burn out, then the ♁ <term>Antimony</term> will be red. When you have crushed this mass finely, half as much ☿ <term>Mercury</term> sublimate <note>A purified form of mercury, likely mercuric chloride, which easily transitions from solid to vapor.</note> shall be crushed into it. Place this then for 15 days in a damp cellar in a shallow earthenware dish, and so it will become moist. Afterwards, place it in a Retort <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-curving neck used for distilling liquids or "driving over" spirits.</note>, and drive it over according to common practice, then a white spirit will come over, which will fill the Receiver <note>The Dutch "Voorligger" refers to the glass vessel that catches the distilled product.</note> as if it were full of milk. This work shall be continued with the ∆ <term>Fire</term> <note>Alchemical symbol for Fire.</note> until all the spirit has passed over and the Receiver is clear. Then let the ∆ <term>Fire</term> go out, and all the spirit has passed over.

When the Retort is cold, one shall take it out of the sand bath <note>The Dutch "zand Capelle" or "sand chapel" is a container of sand used to distribute heat evenly around the glass vessel.</note> and break it into pieces. The *Caput Mortuum* <note>original: "Caput Mortuum". Latin for "dead head," referring to the solid residue or "dregs" left behind after distillation.</note> that remains at the bottom, you must crush finely, and as previously taught, place it on a dish in a cellar, so it will run into an *Aqua* <term>Water</term> or *Oly* <term>Oil</term>, which with

### *Another way to make an Oil or Butter, without the ☿ [Mercury] Sublimate from ♁ [Antimony].*

Take [Recipe]: ♁ [Antimony] *Mineral*. Let this be crushed small, and then a 🜄 [Water] poured upon it, so it dissolves between white and yellow. When the ♁ [Antimony] is then dissolved, then distill the *Water* <note>original: "Aqua"</note> off from it, until it becomes dry, with gentle 🜂 [Fire]. Let your glass then become cold, and take it thus out of the sand-bath <note>original: "zand-Capelle"; a heat-conducting container filled with sand used to provide uniform heat to a glass vessel.</note>. Weigh the *Dead Head* <note>original: "Caput Mortuum"; the solid, spent residue remaining in the vessel after distillation or sublimation.</note> that is at the bottom, how much it may be in weight; against which you shall then add 2 parts of ⊖ [Salt] *Common*, and crush and rub that together. After that, put everything into a *Retort* <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances.</note>, and drive it in the same manner as one makes the Oil of ♁ [Antimony] that is driven through ☿ [Mercury] *Sublimate*. This is almost as good as that which is made through the ☿ [Mercury]. One can "kill" <note>In alchemy, to "kill" a substance often means to fix it (make it non-volatile) or to alter its metallic nature so it can be used medicinally.</note> it through a *Salt* 🜹 [Sal ammoniac], when one wishes to have it from there, and use it as a *Medicine* in the human body: but if one wishes to use it externally against bad wounds, then one must not [weaken] its strength...

...given, now I shall deal with the good signs which are partly certain.

### *Of the Signs and colors of Minerals, as they are revealed through the labor of Miners in hewing.*

1. One shall take heed of the beautiful colors, such as <term>Cinnabar</term> <note>original: Sinober; a bright red mineral which is the primary ore of mercury</note> and <term>Mountain-red</term> <note>original: Berg-rood; likely red ochre or hematite</note>: these give a sign that Gold or Silver must be present nearby.

2. <term>Orpiment</term> <note>original: Auripigment; a deep orange-yellow arsenic sulfide mineral once used as a pigment</note> and Red <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍; the active, combustible principle</note> have the colors of gold-yellow dyes, which are sometimes mixed together; and these commonly contain good <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; the sun</note>.

3. <term>Lapis Lazuli</term> <note>original: Lazuur-steen; an azure-blue stone</note>, marked with <term>Mountain-green</term> <note>original: berg-groen; likely Malachite</note>, and Orpiment: when these three are found together, one has a certain sign that Gold ore is at hand.

4. If one encounters any stones that are red-yellow, it indicates that <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; the planet/god Mars</note> is nearby.

5. When one discovers or touches <term>Verdigris</term> <note>original: Spaens-groen; literally "Spanish green," a green pigment often found as a crust on copper</note> or Mountain-green, it is a certain sign of Copper ore.

6. When one finds a golden Sulfur that has green spots, it gives a sign of Iron or <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀; the planet/god Venus</note>, or both of them together in one mass.

7. Through the <term>Water</term> <note>original: ▽▽; though the triangles point upward—usually the symbol for fire—the context of "running out of the mountains" indicates mountain streams or runoff</note> that flows out of the mountains, one also perceives what kind of minerals are hidden therein; just as one can read in my previous writings.

8. If one also finds thin leaves of ore hanging on the stones, that is one of the surest signs of a good ore; and if it appears like <term>Talc</term> <note>original: talk; likely referring to soapstone or mica-like minerals that often accompany ore</note>, that is also not bad at all.

9. When a <term>Vein</term> <note>original: gank; a seam of ore-bearing rock</note> reveals itself in a firm clay or black earth, and is hidden therein, it also gives a good sign of a mineral; especially when that earth is dry and surrounded by a firm stone: and if it then falls into the depths in such a manner, it is a certain and sure sign that one has ore at hand. I have found this to be so in the <term>West Indies</term> <note>A broad term for the Americas in the 17th century</note> at the latitude of 47 degrees. <note>47 degrees North latitude corresponds to regions like the mouth of the St. Lawrence River or modern-day Washington State/Newfoundland; a significant distance from the Caribbean.</note>

10. When the vein is discovered in the opening, having a brown <term>Liver-colored</term> <note>original: lever-kouleur-achtigen; a reddish-brown hue often associated with iron or copper ores</note> stone, one has a good and certain sign that ore is present there.

11. If one happens to encounter a coarse sand in the mine, that is a good sign; because the same is often found near <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; the sun</note>; just as I have experienced myself in the West Indies and other places.

If one encounters coarse stone that has the color of a <term>glass-ore</term> <note>original: glas-eerts; likely referring to vitreous or shiny minerals like argentite or galena</note>, and lies mixed with small pebbles, that is a certain sign of discovering <term>silver-ore</term> in that same <term>mine</term> <note>original: Berg-werk</note>.

14. Let this be a conclusion and a certain mark: wherever the stone is cloaked with a nobility of colors in the veins and passages, and where it ultimately descends into the depths, this is one of the most excellent signs and shall be held as a treasure; for by the colors one shall recognize—when they are treated by the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>—what was hidden in the earth. Yet concerning these and similar signs, *Paracelsus* <note>The influential 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist</note> also writes in his book *On the Nature of Things* <note>original Latin title: de Natura Rerum</note>; wherefore I shall leave it at this for now, for reasons that move me to do so.

Now I shall return again to our <term>Saturn</term> <note>original symbol: ♄; in alchemy, this usually represents Lead, but here it refers to the "Prime Matter" or the base material of the Great Work</note>, who is clothed with a green coat, under which are a blue doublet and a white shirt; and from his body comes a red blood.

This Saturn is also called <term>Mars</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; Iron</note>, as also sometimes <term>Venus</term> <note>original symbol: ♀; Copper</note>, and <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; Quicksilver</note>, and often <term>Sol</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; Gold</note>, <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: *, ♁; the asterisk often denotes the "star" of antimony</note>, and by many more other names; because his body consists of the <term>Elements</term>, such as <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> and <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, of which I have consistently treated in my writings.

# The Green Lion,

The spirit of the world is a salt, which is produced by the Sun and Moon of the Philosophers <note>Referring to the celestial influences or the alchemical Gold and Silver</note>, and its power first becomes manifest through the salts of the metals. Whoever now wishes to make this salt of the Sages must not forget the <term>✳</term> <note>A star symbol, likely referring to the "Star of the Sea" or the crystalline structure of certain salts</note>, which has come forth from the sea of the same.

If you wish to work without salt, you shall accomplish nothing: therefore, pay heed to the common salts, which are the keys of the Art <note>Alchemy</note>, with which the <term>Sulphur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> of the metals is killed and made living again.

The <term>Sulphur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> of the metals, of which the Ancients write, has its origin—as far as I can observe—from <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; Mars</note> and <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀; Venus</note>, or <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄; Saturn</note>, and similar metals: therefore the Ancients say that the entire Art proceeds from the metals. *Basilius* <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and author</note> also says the same, page 77: namely, *that from there come forth the seeds of the metals.*

This <term>Sulphur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> of the metals is also called <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> and Salt, and that with right truth. They also call it a gum <note>likely referring to a resinous or sticky consistency during the chemical process</note>, and not without reason: yes, moreover even their Green Lion, the green field, a young maiden who comes forth in purple garments; yet she is nothing other than the <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔; a sulfate mineral or acidic substance</note> of the metals, as also their <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> and Salt.

*Basilius* speaks of this on page 175 with the following words: *The <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; Sol</note> needs not to a...*

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

<margin><term>Salt</term> <note>original symbol: ⊕</note></margin> to be made volatile, and that was in no way the practice or the teaching of the Ancient Philosophers; for it strives against Nature.

I say then, as far as I know, that the <term>Salt</term> <note>original symbol: ⊕</note> of <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; Mars</note> or <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀; Venus</note> has as much <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> as Gold itself. But whoever will not believe this may learn to make the <term>Cross-Salt</term> <note>A term for a purified or crystalline salt often associated with vitriol</note> from Iron and Copper, which shall be made with nothing other than common rain <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ∇</note>, through a Salt of <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: 🜖; sometimes also used for Antimony</note>, which is first opened and then becomes a powder and meal. Whether I now have the right Matter, time will tell; since I can make it for nothing other than Medicines, with which I am content to occupy myself for now: for Medicines are better than Gold.

From <term>Silver</term> <note>original symbol: ☾; the Moon</note> a good drink can be made, and also a Medicine against inflammatory diseases; for its fine nature consists in the Salt of <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: 🜖</note>, and it is also a <term>Tin</term> <note>original symbol: ♃; Jupiter</note>, and a <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, which the air has placed therein through the power of the Sun.

### *Short Regulations for Mining Operations.*

1. A <term>Shaft</term> <note>Dutch: *Schacht*. Here referring to the vertical access to a mine, or allegorically, the deep descent into the heart of matter.</note> or <term>Mine-master</term> <note>Dutch: *Stol-meester*. In mining, this was the official in charge of a drainage tunnel or "stul"; in alchemy, it refers to the practitioner overseeing the purification and "drainage" of impurities from the metal.</note> is bound according to all <term>Mining Laws</term> <note>Dutch: *Berg-rechten*. 2. He shall also sort these same Minerals individually during the excavation, so that the good does not come among the bad, and then no damage occurs during the <term>smelting</term> <note>Dutch: *smelten*. In alchemy, this is the process of liquefying the material to separate the "pure" spirit from the "dross."</note>.
3. There shall be a suitable place where the <term>Ore</term> <note>Dutch: *Eerts* (Erts). The raw material containing the desired metallic "seed."</note> or Mineral shall be brought, and each locked by its number in a separate chest; so that one may know what Minerals are on hand.

5. He is also bound every day to send a <term>sample</term> <note>Dutch: *monster* or *proeve*. A small portion of the work used to test for progress or the presence of the "spirit."</note> or test to his Lord or Master, so that he may know,

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

By using Lead <note>Original symbol: ♄.</note> and <term>Common Salt</term> <note>Original symbol: ⊞; likely referring to common salt (sal commune) or a specific mineral flux.</note> in *equal parts* <note>Original: *ana* (Latin).</note>, a flux is also made to melt iron-bearing ore.

Separating minerals by means of fire <note>Original symbol: 🜂.</note> or a <term>solvent process</term> <note>Original symbol: ℞.</note> is not common practice in the mining works because the <term>chemical acids</term> <note>Original symbols: 🜄 🜄; literally "waters," referring to distilled acids like nitric acid or aqua fortis.</note> are too costly, though one may certainly do so for the sake of precision to discover exactly what may be within the mineral substance. The gentleman *Glauber* <note>Johann Rudolf Glauber (1604–1670), a famous German-Dutch alchemist and apothecary known for his improvements to chemical processes and equipment.</note> has written of this; therefore I shall not discuss it at greater length.

The Gold <note>Original symbol: ☉.</note> mineral, if it is rich, is extracted by Mercury <note>Original symbol: ☿.</note>, which is then driven off by means of a Retort, or perhaps by some other tool, of which I shall write more later as the opportunity arises.

Some press the Mercury, in which the Gold is contained, through a deerskin, and place that which remains in the leather upon a sherd or in an iron pan, and let the remaining Mercury evaporate <note>Original: *verrooken*; literally "to smoke away."</note>, whereby they are rid of it. The Gold that then remains, they place with 10 or 12 parts of Lead <note>Original symbol: ♄.</note> upon the Cupel <note>Original: *Capelle*; a shallow, porous bowl used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals like lead.</note>, and let it work until all the Lead has disappeared and the Gold has become pure. This Regulus <note>Original: *Konink* (literally "King"); the metallic button of purified metal found at the bottom of a crucible or cupel.</note> shall then be separated by a <term>solvent</term> <note>Original symbol: ℞.</note> to determine how much Silver <note>Original symbol: ☽.</note> the Gold contains: and this is indeed the shortest way to separate Gold from fine earth. ### The Green Lion,

...be first roasted <note>Roasting involves heating the ore in air to remove volatile components like sulfur before further processing.</note>, before one adds the Mercury <note>Original symbol: ☿.</note> to it: but he who does not yet know this may do as I have done, and pay his "learning-money" <note>Original: *leer-geld*; an idiom meaning to learn an expensive lesson through one's own mistakes.</note> to the Art <note>Referring to the "Art of Alchemy" or metallurgy.</note>.

Silver ore is treated with Lead <note>Original symbol: ♄.</note>, having first been roasted, and afterwards washed and dried; then let it be refined upon a <term>cupel</term> <note>Original: *Capelle*; a shallow, porous bowl used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals like lead.</note> with 6 to 8 parts [of lead], so that one finds how much the mineral contains. But you must first test the Lead to see how much Silver a pound of Lead contains <note>Lead often naturally contains trace amounts of silver; the assayer must subtract this "background" silver to find the true value of the ore being tested.</note>, so that you may rightly know what is in the mineral.

Copper <note>Original symbol: ♀.</note>, Silver <note>Original symbol: ☽.</note>, Lead <note>Original symbol: ♄.</note>, Tin <note>Original symbol: ♃.</note>, and Gold <note>Original symbol: ☉.</note> can be perfectly tested by the aforementioned powder of Sal Ammoniac <note>Original symbol: 🜔.</note>, Alum <note>Original symbol: 🜖.</note>, Saltpetre <note>Original symbol: ⊖.</note>, glass-gall <note>A scum of impurities that forms on the surface of molten glass, often used as a flux in smelting.</note>, and iron filings, as was previously related. The minerals must also first be well roasted together, and then placed in the <term>Water</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜄; likely referring to a corrosive acid or solvent.</note>, or have it poured upon them; and then once again in the furnace, for as long as any sulfurous stench is perceived from them. In this way, the "evil salts" shall also be removed, such as Sal Ammoniac <note>Original symbol: 🜔.</note> and the Gold <note>Original symbol: ☉. While the symbol for Gold is used here, the context of "evil salts" that "rob" the metal suggests the author may be referring to a specific mineral impurity or a volatile "unripe" form of the metal lost to slag.</note>, which are harmful in the smelting; for these salts create much <term>slag</term> <note>Original: *ſlakken*; waste material produced during the smelting of ore.</note> in the work, and also "rob" the metal. These salts cannot be removed without roasting, of which more has been treated in my previous writings.

### To separate Copper, Iron, and Silver from one another.

Recipe. Take mineral as previously described, and add to it a mineral of Lead <note>Original symbol: ♄.</note> that is believed to contain Lead and Sulfur <note>Original symbol: 🜍.</note>. Add this to the aforementioned ore, up to 10 parts, and melt it together; thus that Lead will draw in the Copper <note>Original symbol: ♀.</note> and Silver <note>Original symbol: ☽.</note>, and the Iron <note>Original symbol: ♂.</note> will become slag. This <term>regulus</term> <note>Original: *Konink*; literally "king." In metallurgy, this refers to the metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible, separated from the slag.</note> shall then be placed with 10 parts of pure Lead upon a hearth; then the Lead draws all the Copper away from the Silver, so that one then has the Silver in a refined state, from which one can separate the Gold by means of <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>Original symbol: ∇; literally "water," but in this context referring to nitric acid, the "strong water" used to dissolve silver and leave gold behind.</note>, which has been dealt with in my other writings.

One can melt the Copper ore or mineral once it has first been well burnt-out or roasted <note>Heating the ore to drive off volatile impurities like sulfur or arsenic.</note>, and that in the same manner as one melts Lead or Iron, without the addition of any powders, using only a blast furnace <note>Original: *stijg-oven*; a furnace where air is forced through the fuel to reach higher temperatures.</note> by blowing [air] into charcoal; however, more can be read about this in other mining books <note>Original: *Berg-boeken*; specialized texts on geology, mining, and smelting.</note>.

I shall now return again to the works of the Philosophers, who treat of medicines and various other matters.

In *The Golden Lion*, page 83, I have written of a *Milk of Sulfur* <note>Original: *Lac 🜍* (*Lac Sulphuris*); a fine, white precipitate of sulfur used in early modern medicine.</note> produced from common Sulfur...

# The Green Lion,

<meta>catchword: word</meta>

...can do no benefit, unless the innermost being must first be driven out, as seen on page 31. And the same is also experienced in all other things. Therefore, *Basil Valentine* <note>original: "Basilius"; a legendary 15th-century alchemist, though his works likely appeared later</note> writes the following regarding <term>Venus ♀; the metal copper and its associated planetary spirit</term> in his rhymes on page 141:

> *From Venus's body make a stone,*
> *And drive out from it the spirit alone,*
> *Entirely red and muddy like blood,*
> *Which makes Mars break apart entirely.*
> *Again, make from that a stone,*
> *Corresponding with the first.*
> *Herein lies a wondrously great art,*
> *When Luna is clothed out of favor.*
> *Indeed, Sol can do nothing more without him;*
> *This strikes Mercury down as if dead.*
> *If you can order these matters rightly,*
> *They shall then pass the judgment.*

Here he has spoken of the spirits, and says that one shall seek them from the Metals and their Salts, and not take the whole Matter for the <term>Philosophical Salt; the purified essence of a substance, considered the "key" to transmutation</term>, but rather the spirit that dwells within the raw <term>Body; original: "Corpus"; the unrefined physical matter of a metal or mineral</term>, and that this is their Salt.

The philosopher *Mundus* <note>likely referring to a character in alchemical dialogues or the "Turba Philosophorum"</note> speaks on page 23 of this spirit, and calls it a <term>Virgin’s Milk; original: "Jonkvrouw-melk"; a symbolic term for a white, nourishing solvent or precipitate</term>.

*The Afflicted Philosopher* <note>original: "Afflictus Philosophus"</note> gives it the name of a <term>Water-vinegar; original: "Water-asijn"; likely a translation of "Acetum fontis," a sharp, acidic solvent used in the "dissolving" stage of the work</term>, which becomes dry in 40 days...

# A L L E G O R Y

## Of the Most Holy Trinity concerning the Philosopher’s Stone.

O dear Christian lovers of this blessed Art: how has the Holy Trinity created the Philosopher’s Stone <note>original: Lapidem Philofophorum</note> so gloriously and also so wonderfully! For God the Father is a spirit, and yet lets himself be seen in the form of a man; as we read in his Word, Genesis, in the 1st chapter, where he speaks: *Let us make man, an image that is like unto us.* And elsewhere he lets human parts be attributed to him; such as mouth, eyes, hands, and feet. And so is the Mercury <note>original: ☿</note> of the Philosophers also to be considered a spiritual body <note>original: spirituale Corpus</note>, as the Ancients have likewise named it. From God the Father his only Son Jesus Christ is born, who is also God, and man without sin; and had no need to die, but died voluntarily, and rose again, for the sake of his brothers and sisters, so that they might live with him eternally. In the same way is Gold <note>original: ☉, the Sun</note> without any defect, and fixed <note>referring to its ability to withstand fire without evaporating</note>, enduring through all kinds of trials, and glorious; but for the sake of its imperfect, sick brothers and sisters <note>the "lesser" or base metals like lead or tin</note>, it dies voluntarily, and afterwards rises gloriously and joyfully, and tinctures <note>a term for the alchemical process of transforming the inner nature or "color" of a substance</note> them unto eternal life, and makes them into a perfect and good Gold <note>original: ☉</note>. O dear Christian lovers of this blessed Art: how gloriously and also wonderfully has the Holy Trinity created the *Philosopher's Stone* <note>original: "Lapidem Philosophorum"</note>! For *God the Father* is a spirit, and yet lets himself be seen in the form of a man; as we read in his Word, *Genesis*, in the 1st Chapter. Where he speaks: "Let us make humans, an image that is like unto us." And elsewhere he allows human parts to be ascribed to himself; such as mouth, eyes, hands, and feet. And so the *Mercury of the Philosophers* <note>symbol: ☿</note> is also to be considered a *spiritual body* <note>original: "spirituale Corpus"</note>, just as the Ancients have named him. From *God the Father* was his only Son *Jesus Christ* born, who is also God, and man without sin; and who had no need to die, but died voluntarily and rose again for the sake of his brothers and sisters, so that they might live with him eternally. # or the Light of the Philosophers. 139

[it] also teaches in part the operation to prepare the same; yet not so clearly that all the world shall understand it: so that it is made from the Salts and Metals. Of these Salts now, from which such Water <note>symbol: ∇</note> and Fire <note>symbol: Δ</note> ought to be made, I believe I have revealed far too much to the ungrateful world in my writings.

In the *Golden Lion* I have taught how one can draw the *Anima* <term>Anima: the "soul" or essential spirit of a substance, believed to be its most potent part</term> out of the Metals and other things; but if one lets the Metals stand for some months with the vinegar <note>symbol: +; usually referring to distilled vinegar or a corrosive acid used as a solvent</note>, and then distills them, one shall obtain a wondrous Fire <note>symbol: Δ; here referring to a concentrated spirit or an "oil" that possesses heating properties</note>; of which I shall write further hereafter, and show in what manner one shall treat certain Metals to obtain their Oil, Sublimation <note>symbol: ♎</note>, Essence <note>symbol: 🜪; likely a purified metallic distillate</note>, and Salt <note>symbol: 🜔</note>.

# 138 The Green Lion,

The person in the Holy Trinity is God the Holy Spirit, a comforter from our Lord Jesus Christ, who strengthens and comforts his faithful Christians, so that they believe in him for eternal life. So it is also with the spirit and the Matter of the Mercury <term>Mercury: represented by the symbol ☿, referring here to the "mercurial" or fluidic essence of matter</term> Body: for when they come together, it is then called a doubled Mercury <term>Mercury duplicatus: a "doubled" mercury, indicating a substance that has been joined with its own internal spirit or another metallic essence</term>, which are then two spirits: God the Father, and God the Holy Spirit; but God the Son is the Glorified Man <note>original: "Homo glorificatus"</note>, resembling our own glorified and fixed Gold <note>symbol: ☉</note> of the Philosophers' Stone <note>original: "Lapis Philosophorum"</note>. Therefore this Stone is also called Threefold <note>original: "Trinus"</note>, namely originating from two Waters or Spirits, the Mineral and the Vegetable, to which an Animal Fire <note>symbol: △; the "animal" refers to the third kingdom of nature, often associated with the soul or blood</note> Gold <note>symbol: ☉</note> is added. These are then two and three, and yet only one. If you do not understand this, you shall achieve nothing. Thus I have sufficiently described the Universal <note>The "Universal" refers to the Great Work or the fundamental medicine that applies to all things</note> to you through comparison: pray to God for his blessing; for without him you shall find nothing. Amen.

## *Of the Oak Tree.*

The oak wood that has borne acorns every year and is fruitful; this shall serve as your Matter. Let the branches be hewn from the trunk, and then you must strip the bark from them, and dry the same bark in a baker's oven. You shall then place the thickest end of the branches into a wood fire, and place a glass receiver <note>original: *Voorligger*; a vessel placed at the end of a distillation apparatus to collect the condensed vapors</note> at the other end, in order to catch the spirit <note>original symbol: ♎︎ (Libra); in this context of wood distillation, it refers to the acidic "spirit" or pyroligneous acid</note> which comes out of the wood as a white mist, which you shall let run down into the glass in drops. When this piece is finished distilling, throw it into the fire <note>original symbol: △</note>, and take another. This you must do until you have enough spirit to carry out your work properly. After that, take 4 pounds of this spirit <note>original: ♎︎</note> which has come out of the wood, and pour it onto one pound of powder from the same dried bark, crushed in an iron mortar; thus the red soul <note>The "soul" in alchemy refers to the internal essence or tincture—the "sulfur" of the plant</note> shall be extracted from the bark in a very short time, which you must then filter through a blotting paper; then this shall [be] so

In that same book, on page 79, I have dealt with making a good <term>brandy [brandewijn]</term> <note>literally "burnt wine," referring to distilled spirits or alcohol</note> from all tree-fruits and plants of the earth, which could not be improved upon. But the Art is to open the house of <term>Mercury [☿]</term> before you shall be able to obtain his Salt and <term>Antimony [♁]</term>; from which you may then learn to make the true <term>Mercury [☿]</term> and *Vinegar of the Philosophers* <note>A potent alchemical solvent, often not literal vinegar</note>, in which I myself am not yet a Master.

In the *Red Lion*, page 71, I have told of a Philosopher with whom I labored in the year 1660 within *Nijmegen*: now, for the instruction of others, I shall describe what state and progress our *Labors* <note>original: "Labores," referring to alchemical experiments or works</note> had there.

At first, our materials were of many kinds, but we began with common <term>Mercury [☿]</term>, which we then purified through <term>Vinegar [🜊]</term> and <term>Salt [🜔]</term>, as is customary according to the teaching of...

Whatever else there may be to this is entirely unknown to me: yet I have often held the Stone in my hand, which was very heavy, like Gold itself, and transparent, like a Ruby. I have also used some of that Medicine myself, which he administered to me.

# The Green Lion,
<note>In alchemy, the "Green Lion" usually refers to Vitriol (iron or copper sulfate) or the raw, acidic mineral matter used to dissolve gold.</note>

He spoke these words about an hour before his death, and no one was with him except for me and my wife; and shortly thereafter, he passed away.

Based on these words that I had now learned from him, I worked thereafter in <term>Gold [☉]</term>, but obtained nothing—perhaps because I did not yet know the "opening" of it. Yet now I know very well that its spirit opens, and its <term>Fire [△]</term> or <term>Alkahest</term> <note>original: "Alkeſt"; a hypothetical universal solvent capable of dissolving every other substance</note> seals itself in <note>original: "toefluit"; likely referring to the hermetic sealing of a vessel or the inward flow of the substance</note>, and thereafter opens again.

Common Salt precipitates the <term>Silver [☽]</term>.

# The Green Lion,

...those where the grounds are silty <note>original: "filtachtig." The <term>Common Salt [🜔]</term> is also a salt, but does not have the nature of the Salts of the Metals, for those are brought by this **Vegetable Salt** <note>Alchemists distinguished between "vegetable" salts derived from plants/organic matter and "mineral" salts from the earth.</note>, into a <term>Sulfur [🜍]</term> and **Calx** <note>A powder or residue left after a metal has been burned or "calcined."</note>, of which I have treated on *pages 51 and 52 in the Cabinet* <note>Likely referring to the author’s other work, "Het Kabinet der Mineralen" (The Cabinet of Minerals).</note>.

The **Metals** each have a conflicting nature against one another, such as <term>Lead [♄]</term> against <term>Tin [♃]</term>, <term>Mercury [☿]</term> against <term>Copper [♀]</term> and <term>Iron [♂]</term>, and so forth with the others.

The Salts are also in the same degree; as, <term>Alum [🜪]</term> is conflicting against <term>Common Salt [🜔]</term> and <term>Sal-ammoniac [✳]</term>: the *Alkali* are enemies to all distilled salt-spirits: the **quicklime** <note>Calcium oxide, which reacts violently with water and acids.</note> is conflicting against all acidic things, which it kills through its <term>Fire [🜂]</term>, and brings into a sweeter essence.

### or the Light of the Philosophers.
<meta>catchword: than</meta>

...than the **Lion** <note>original: "Leeuw." Likely referring to the "Green Lion," a common alchemical symbol for vitriol or a strong mineral acid capable of dissolving gold.</note> yet treated. There I have also, on page 100, written of the **City of Hermes** <note>original: "Stad Hermes." A metaphorical "place" representing a specific state of chemical composition or a stable laboratory environment where the "Great Work" occurs.</note>, which is nothing other than the Salts, which through **Calcination** <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures in the presence of air to drive off volatile matter or reduce it to a powder.</note> run into a Water, and thereafter become two Rivers, with which one can open the Metals and release them from their bonds: and this Salt is also called **Gold**.

# The Green Lion,

...then will be used for our work of Medicines.

In <term>Iron [♂]</term>, there is Salt and <term>Sulfur [🜍]</term>.
In <term>Copper [♀]</term>, there is <term>Sulfur [🜍]</term> and <term>Mercury [☿]</term>.
In <term>Mercury [☿]</term>, there dwells the <term>Sal Ammoniac [✳]</term> and Earth.
In <term>Silver [☽]</term>, there is a Salt and Earth.
In <term>Gold [☉]</term> is the seed, and that same [metal] receives the tincture of <term>Copper [♀]</term> and <term>Iron [♂]</term>.

This is that which the Ancient Sages make known to us in their Books: therefore *Paracelsus* speaks in his *Cœlum Philoſophorum* <note>Latin: "The Heaven of the Philosophers," a famous alchemical treatise by Paracelsus (1493–1541).</note>, page 386, with these words:

> *Gold is in three types of state and being: Heavenly, Elementary, and Metallic; that is, dissolving, melting, and lovely; so that Gold then is all in all, just as common Mercury [☿] is also.*

Therefore I say, that in <term>Mercury [☿]</term> is <term>Sulfur [🜍]</term> and earth, which can also be called a Salt <term>Ammoniac [✳]</term>. This is brought to light with the common <term>Sal Ammoniac [✳]</term> when the **Cold Dragon** <note>The "Cold Dragon" often refers to crude saltpeter or a specific mineral vapor that "fixes" or reacts with mercury.</note> comes to it, and then it is first recognized.

The **Alkalis** <note>original: "Alcali." Referring to basic salts derived from the ashes of plants or calcined minerals.</note> are all Salts that have been calcined <note>Reduced to powder by heat.</note> and sublimated <note>Purified by turning into vapor and condensing back into solid form.</note> by <term>Fire [🜂]</term>; and we understand by the calcination of Salts both those of Metals and Minerals, and also of Stones, from which one burns a Lime, which may be leached through <term>Water [🜄]</term> or Vinegar into a Salt, which one can then take for one's use.

### How to make an Oil of Tartar.

Take 4 pounds of <term>Antimony [♁]</term> <note>A brittle, silvery-gray metalloid often used in alchemy as a starting point for purifying metals or creating medicinal "oils."</note>; this shall be burned out in a charcoal <term>Fire [Δ]</term> for a whole hour, until it becomes green. When it then comes out of the <term>Fire [Δ]</term>, it must be placed upon a glass table; in a short time, it shall run into an oil, or melt of its own accord. This oil contains a great <term>Fire [Δ]</term> within it, which can be extracted from it by means of <term>Sal-ammoniac [*]</term> <note>A volatile mineral salt used as a flux or solvent.</note> or Urine. Of this process, it is written in the *Red Lion* <note>Likely a reference to a specific alchemical treatise or a stage in the "Great Work" where the matter turns red.</note>; but not of the <term>calcination</term> <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or change its chemical state.</note>. With this spirit, one can do much in medicine; and one could also open all the <term>Sulfurs [🜍]</term> <note>The "Sulfur" of a metal was considered its internal combustible "soul" or essence.</note> of the metals with it.

Note Well: The stone-lime also does it no harm in this opening, to obtain its <term>Fire [Δ]</term> or spirit; and of this, others have also partly written. Thereafter, this spirit is mixed with <term>Spirit of Wine [Ω]</term> <note>Highly concentrated alcohol produced through repeated distillation.</note>, and then placed upon the metallic <term>Sulfur [🜍]</term> for a short time and distilled over; thus you obtain the true <term>Mercury Water [Aqua ☿]</term> <note>A philosophical solvent used to dissolve metals, distinct from common liquid mercury.</note> that is to be drawn from vegetable and animal substances. He who can make this shall well obtain its use: however, in the *Red Lion*, this has also been dealt with.

Instead of such medicines being loved by everyone, one finds

The Green Lion,

I [observe] that many despise these same things, saying that the medicines made through the **Philosophical art** <note>A common term for Alchemy, emphasizing its status as a natural philosophy.</note> are dangerous to use; which contradicts natural reason: for the Philosophers purge their medicines through the power of <term>Fire [$\triangle$]</term> <note>The triangle is the alchemical symbol for the element of Fire.</note>, which is the highest purification in Nature, and everything brought forth by <term>Fire [$\triangle$]</term> is perfected.

In the **Barbarian Lands** <note>A term used by 17th-century writers to refer to non-European territories.</note>, where they know of no Doctors, the inhabitants have the knowledge that they shall use no medicines unless they first purify them through <term>Fire [$\triangle$]</term> before they seek their healing through them; which I myself have seen: for which reason I judge those people more sensible than many here in this country; since they know that <term>Fire [$\triangle$]</term> purges everything. If a thing is to be used as medicine, it must first be rightly prepared for it by <term>Fire [$\triangle$]</term>, otherwise it is no medicine, but rather poison, which can help people to death more easily than it can make them healthy.

Among these **Heathen Indians** <note>Likely referring to indigenous peoples in the Americas or the East Indies encountered during the author's travels.</note> I have seen many other noteworthy things, which I judge will not be unpleasant to the readers; therefore I wish to share the same with them: and first, how they treat their enemies when they have taken them prisoner.

<meta>This page continues the author's ethnographic diversion, moving from the philosophical justification of fire-based medicine to a graphic description of the ritual torture practices he observed among the indigenous peoples he calls "Heathen Indians."</meta>

->or the Light of the Philosophers.<-

When they then bring a captured enemy <note>original: *Vyand*</note> into their camp, they all come to bid him welcome, and their children make him sit down upon a large stone, also welcoming him; and every young boy then comes to give him a number of blows. By the count of these strikes, the time is signified as to how long he shall be permitted to live before he meets his death. Should they strike him 10, 12, 15, 25, or more times, then for that many days they intend to visit him before his life must reach its end.

I have observed that for one who was destined to live ten or twelve days, they returned on the second day and cut off his thumbs and middle fingers; they then left him sitting as before, giving him food to eat.

On the third day, they cut off both of his ears and again gave him something to eat.

On the fourth day, they forced onto him a pair of "stockings" made from the bark of trees, which they then set on fire while on his legs; all of this he was expected to endure while laughing <note>Early modern travel accounts frequently remarked on the ritual stoicism and "defiant laughter" of indigenous captives during execution rituals.</note>.

On the fifth day, they cut the skin loose around his head and peeled it off with all the hair intact, like a wig <note>original: *paruik*</note>, which they then hang high in their houses as an ornament <note>original: *cieraed*</note>. And as soon as they have removed the scalp from his head, they take warm sand and strew it there—

On the sixth day, they cut off all the toes of his feet and strewed the wounds with hot sand.

On the seventh day, they further cut off all his fingers, yet continued to give him food to eat every day.

On the eighth day, they cut off his nose and tore open his entire jawbone <note>original: *kinnebakken*</note>, letting it hang down upon his chest, which was a most pitiable thing to behold.

On the ninth day, they cut the tongue out of his mouth.

On the tenth, they cut off his male member.

On the eleventh, they made a large stone very glowing hot, upon which they set him with his buttocks; and still perceiving some life in him, they pierced him with wooden awls <note>original: *prie-men*; sharp, pointed tools for piercing small holes</note>.

On the twelfth day, they cut off his cranium or brain-pan <note>original: *bekkeneel of herſſen-pan*</note>, from which they fashioned a drinking bowl <note>The "skull cup" is a recurring motif in early modern travelogues to signify the perceived "otherness" and cruelty of indigenous peoples.</note>, out of which they all drank together; and immediately they went to roast his flesh and eat of it, both the small and great persons alike. Over this they performed their song, which I could not understand.

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

This cruel treatment occurs at a latitude of 47 degrees North of the Line <note>The Equator</note>.

In the year 1663, I was also at a latitude of 4 degrees, with my wife and three young sons, who all three died and were buried there due to the country-sickness <term>Land-ſiekte: a general term used by Dutch colonists for tropical illnesses, likely malaria or yellow fever</term>; and I made this journey in the company of the Right Honorable Mr. *Quirijn Spranger*, Director and Governor of those same lands.

The Indians in that country are large and heavy of body, handsome, and well-proportioned in all things, brown of hue, living in houses made of the leaves of trees, which leaves are five to six feet long and two feet wide. They all go about naked, both men and women. If they capture any of their neighbors, all of whom they consider to be enemies, they cut a piece of meat from his buttocks, roast it, and eat it; afterward, they hang him by the arms from a tree, about 2 feet from the ground, and then build a large fire under his feet, roasting him until he is dead; finally, they burn him entirely to ashes,

These lands are very pleasant, if only well-behaved, reasonable people lived there. They have ships or vessels there made from hollow trees, in which as many as 30 or more persons can sit together, and thus travel with them from one place or island to another. The men do nothing else but go on the hunt with their bows and arrows. They are, however, also alchemists: for they know how to calcine <term>calcine: to reduce a substance to ash or powder through heat</term> their stones by means of <term>△</term> <note>Alchemical symbol for fire</note>, from which they then make certain dyes with which they paint their entire bodies instead of clothing them. Through their lower lips, they have a thread from which a pearl hangs, and through the nose are two holes where they stick parrot feathers, as well as in their ears, so that one gazes upon them with wonder. When the women are delivered of a child, the men observe the lying-in <note>A reference to the *couvade*, a custom where the father ritualistically takes to his bed following a birth</note> for them, and the woman must do everything that needs to be done. ->or the Light of the Philosophers.<-

Their bread is made from the roots of small trees <note>Referring to the cassava or manioc plant</note>, which roots they peel, which are thereafter grated small, and then they press out the liquid, which is very poisonous; indeed, if an animal were to drink of it, it would die, as also happened to my dog. The remainder having been dried into a flour, they then make their bread from it, quite flat like cakes. Furthermore, they have another kind of grain, called *Mylie* <note>original: *Mylie*, likely referring to maize or corn</note>, which they also use for bread, but this is not considered as good as the first. Their drink they make from fruits grown under the earth, called *Pataes* <note>original: *Pataes*, sweet potatoes</note>: these are earth-apples <note>A literal translation of the Dutch *aard-appelen*, here meaning sweet potatoes</note>, very good of taste. The old women without teeth first chew these small in their mouths and spit it out into a vessel; and after it is 10 or 12 days old, they pour water upon it, which is boiled, and afterwards it ferments <note>original: *werkt het in sich selven*, literally "works within itself"</note> like beer; and this is the Indians' drink, which is so strong that one can certainly become drunk from it if one drinks it heartily. They also have pineapple wine, and cashew drink <note>original: *Kajouwen-drank*</note>, along with several others.

There are enormously many trees which bear both chestnuts and apples at the same time <note>A description of the cashew tree (*Anacardium occidentale*), where the nut (the "chestnut") grows outside the fleshy fruit (the "apple")</note>, sour of taste. One also has there orange-apples <note>Oranges</note>—

Goats are also there in great number. There are also an abundance of *iguanas*, which are good to eat; these have a long snout with a very long tail, terrifying to behold, and they can run tremendously fast.

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

Throughout the fields and the forests, many different kinds of *monkeys* run. The trees everywhere are also full of *parrots* and *ravens* <note>Likely referring to large black tropical birds or perhaps vultures, which early explorers often misidentified using European names</note> with very beautiful feathers of various colors. Across the land run a very great multitude of *geese*, of which some are red and others are white <note>The "red geese" are likely scarlet ibises or West Indian flamingos, which would have been striking to a European traveler</note>. *Snipes* are there in great multitudes; and one also has very large and broad *frogs* and *toads* there, as wide as a man's foot. One finds many very large *snakes* there, of 10, 12, 15, 20, even up to 30 feet long, very terrifying to behold: these also eat humans when they can catch them <note>Likely referring to the green anaconda or large boa constrictors</note>. There also fly very large *bats*, which come to bite the ears, nose, and toes of people's feet at night when they do not cover themselves properly in their hammocks <note>A description of the common vampire bat (*Desmodus rotundus*), which often targeted early colonists sleeping in the open</note>. There are also very many large *scorpions*, by one of which I myself was stung on my hand during the night, which caused my whole arm to swell: the pain from it lasted three days before it passed entirely; indeed, I thought I would have died from its intensity; but the *Golden Panacea* <note>original: *Panacea Aurea*; in alchemy, this refers to a universal medicine, often a tincture of gold, believed to cure all ailments</note> preserved me, by God's grace, otherwise it would have been the end of me; for the hot burning I felt was unbearable. There are also very large *grasshoppers*, and some *flies* which by night...

They are entirely red of body, burning like a fire when one touches them <note>These are almost certainly Fire Ants (*Solenopsis*), known for their painful, burning stings and highly organized foraging trails</note>. When they are on land, they can be caught by two or three men, each having a large stick in his hand, which they thrust under the **turtle** and thereby flip it onto its back, which must then remain lying so. In one night, as many as fifty were caught by our people.

Near the **Amazon River** is a certain place where **sea-cows** <note>original: *Koey-beesten*; these are Manatees (*Trichechus*), aquatic mammals often described by early explorers as "sea cows" due to their herbivorous nature and size</note> come up out of the sea, which, being caught by the Indians, some were brought to us. It is also a very fine meat to eat. One finds there every kind of **fish** that one might imagine; among which is a kind called ***Bagger*** <note>Likely the *Bagre* or large catfish common to South American rivers, known for their white flesh</note>, five or six feet long, very beautiful and white, and of an exceptionally sweet flavor. There is also a kind of small fish that fly out of the water, and when their wings are dry, they must fall back into the water: these are very abundant. There is another kind of ***eel***, and if one merely touches it, a person becomes paralyzed in their arms and legs for twenty-four hours, after which he regains his strength; and yet they are good to eat <note>The author is describing the Electric Eel (*Electrophorus electricus*), though the claim of 24-hour paralysis is a common period exaggeration of the numbing sensation caused by its shock</note>.

# The Green Lion,

<image-desc>An alchemical engraving. On the left, a winged cupid-like figure stands in profile, aiming a bow and arrow towards a man on the right. The cupid is marked with a triangle (symbolizing Fire) on its torso and an inverted triangle (symbolizing Water) on its leg; clouds of smoke or vapor rise from its head. On the right, a man in 17th-century European dress (a doublet, breeches, and a plumed hat) stands with one hand to his forehead in a gesture of distress. He is marked with the symbol for Saturn (♄) on his leg. Between and around them are various planetary and alchemical symbols: above are the Sun (☉), Mars (♂), and Jupiter (♃). To the far right is a vertical column of symbols: Moon (☽), Venus (♀), Mercury (☿), and a cross over a circle (likely Earth or Antimony). Below them on the ground are several symbols including Venus (♀), a variation of Jupiter/Tin, and others resembling squared circles or crosses.</image-desc>

> Ah! Venus’s deceitful appearance <note>Original: *looſe ſchijn*. This refers to the deceptive beauty or "glimmer" of the goddess Venus, who in alchemy represents the metal Copper or the seductive nature of the initial transformative stages</note>
> Has completely dispirited <note>Original: *ontzielt*; literally "to deprive of soul," meaning to drain of life, vitality, or essence</note> **Mars** <note>Mars represents the metal Iron or the masculine, forceful principle in the work</note>:
> Thus he sits in pain,
> And is destroyed by the **Fire**.
> How could you, **Sun**, and **Moon**,
> Behold this so peacefully? <note>Original: *vreedſaem*. The poet asks why the Sun (Gold) and Moon (Silver) remain calm or indifferent while Mars is consumed</note>

Everything that is in the world is brought forth by **Salt** and **Water** with the help and by means of the **Fire**; otherwise all things in the whole world would be dead.
The **Salt** is the dwelling of the **Fire**, and the fire is the life of the **Salt**; so that those two must become one, just as one can see by the **Nitre** <term>🜔: Nitre or Saltpeter. Alchemists believed Nitre contained a "hidden fire" or "universal spirit" because of its role in combustion</term>, which is a Salt, and yet gives off **Fire** from itself.

<page-num>181</page-num>
## ...or the Light of the Philosophers.

The Metallic **Fire** <note>Original symbol: Δ</note> is no common **Gold** <note>Original symbol: ☉</note>, nor common salt, nor any other **material body** <note>Original symbol: □, often representing the element Earth or a physical salt</note>; but the **plume-salt** <term>Plume-salt (pluim-sout): A crystalline substance, likely lead acetate (Sugar of Lead) given the description of its "sweet taste" and snow-white appearance</term>, very white as snow, sweet of taste, easily melting in common **Fire** <note>Original symbol: Δ</note>, and so forth.

A certain **Philosopher** <note>A common way for alchemists to refer to earlier authorities like Geber or Hermes Trismegistus</note> says, that *nothing has the power to tinge <note>To "tinge" means to fundamentally transform the color and essence of a base metal into gold</note> the Metals, except that which is brought forth by the metallic species, and it must also take its beginning from there.* He does not say that this Salt would be in **Gold** <note>Original symbol: ☉</note> or **Silver** <note>Original symbol: ☽</note>; and yet this same substance is made through Gold and Silver.

The **Mercury** <note>Original symbol: ☿; here referring to the "Mercury of the Philosophers," a spiritual essence rather than common quicksilver</note> comes forth from a lowly thing, and they also call it the Salt of the Metals, because it is made from the Metals through the power of **Fire** <note>Original symbol: Δ</note>.

I say then further, that this Salt comes from the **old and crippled Saturn** <note>Original symbol: ♄. In alchemy, Saturn is the symbol for the metal Lead, often personified as a slow or crippled old man</note>, with the **Spirit of the World** <term>Spirit of the World (*Spiritus Mundi*): A hypothetical universal substance or "breath of life" believed to permeate all matter</term>, which I, in my judgment, find to be most present in **Wine**, in order to open all Metals therewith. Read of this from **Basil Valentine** <note>A famous, likely pseudonymous, 15th-century alchemist whose works were highly influential in the 17th century</note>, in his *Repetition of the Great Stone, pages 96, and 97.* where he says:

> *I must make it known, that this **Antimony** <note>Original symbol: †</note> is not the Vinegar of the Wise; for our Vinegar is another drink, namely the Matter itself: and the Stone of the Wise is made from the Vinegar of the Wise, which must be prepared through the distilled **Vinegar** <note>Original symbol: ☓, which can represent Salt or Vinegar in different systems; given the context of "distilled," it likely refers to acid</note>, and also the spirit of Wine.*

<sig>M 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: Here</meta>

<summary>This page defines the "Metallic Fire" and "Salt of Metals" not as common substances, but as specific alchemical preparations derived from lead (Saturn) and the spirit of wine. It emphasizes that the true transformative agent must come from the metals themselves and references Basil Valentine to distinguish between common vinegar and the philosophical "Vinegar of the Wise."</summary>
<keywords>Metallic Fire, Plume-salt, Sugar of Lead, Saturn, Mercury of the Philosophers, Spirit of the World, Basil Valentine, Vinegar of the Wise, Alchemical Symbols</keywords>

# The Green Lion,

From this it is very well to be seen where one must seek the **Water** <note>Original symbol: ▽</note>: not from the **Dew** or the **Rain**, but in the **Green Lion** <note>The "Green Lion" is a famous alchemical metaphor, often referring to vitriol (iron sulfate) or a powerful solvent like "secret" mercury that can dissolve gold</note>, which is described in the *Golden Lion, page 106, and page 115*; there you shall find what kind of **Water** <note>Original symbol: ▽</note> this is, through which the **Metals** shall be opened. **Count Bernard** <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), an Italian alchemist who sought the philosopher's stone for most of his life</note> calls it his **Fountain**, of which enough can be read in his fourth book.

**Basil** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and monk, though many works under his name were likely written later</note> speaks of the **Wine** and of the **Gold** with the following words, in his *Recapitulations of the Great Stone, page 98.* *Now man loves **Gold** <note>Original symbol: ☉</note> and **Wine** above all other things that can be surveyed with eyes in the world. Note: **Gold** <note>Original symbol: ☉</note> loves Man and Wine; therefore it willingly lets its noblest parts follow into the **spirit of wine** <term>Spirit of wine (Wijn-geest): Ethanol or high-proof alcohol, used here as a solvent to extract the "essence" of gold</term>, so that a drink comes forth from it.* Thus the Wine loves the **Metals** and their **Salts**, of which I shall treat at another time.

All **Metals** proceed from **Fire** <note>Original symbol: △</note> and **Salt**; but their garments are distinct, such that **Mars** <note>Original symbol: ♂ (Iron)</note> has a red coat on, and beneath it is a green and a white one.

**Venus** <note>Original symbol: ♀ (Copper)</note> has a red cloak on, but beneath it are heavenly colors, and her bones are white as ivory.

<summary>This page identifies the 'Green Lion' as the true source of the alchemical solvent rather than natural dew, and discusses the chemical affinity between gold and alcohol (spirit of wine). It also describes the symbolic 'clothing' or hidden internal colors of the metals Mars (iron) and Venus (copper).</summary>

This Vinegar is a most excellent Medicine for purifying all foul Wounds and bringing them to a good healing, of which others have already written enough.

When one makes Salt with this Spirit <note>Original symbol: ✚; likely referring here to the acidic spirit or essence</note> of Antimony <note>Original symbol: ♁</note>, by means of a water of *Alkahest* <note>Original: "Alkes," the hypothetical universal solvent of alchemy</note>, it heals all ailments in the Human Body; for its fine virtue and power cannot be described.

One calcines <note>A process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter or to oxidize it</note> the Salt <note>Original symbol: □</note> by means of Fire <note>Original symbol: △</note>; and with the Antimony <note>Original symbol: ♁</note> it also yields a red Oil if it is placed upon a glass table in a damp cellar <note>This describes deliquescence, where a substance absorbs enough moisture from the air to dissolve and turn into a liquid "oil"</note>.

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

of our God, whom we daily behold in the Art <note>The "Art" (Konst) refers to alchemy, the Great Work</note> of the Philosophers. But all those who wish to work without any instruction will learn to know Nature only with great difficulty: for the works of the Lord are not to be fathomed.

In the first Book of *Moses* <note>original: *Mosis*; the Book of Genesis</note>, in the first Chapter, one reads that the Spirit of God hovered over the waters; and after that, we are told of the separation of the waters which were above the Firmament, and under the Firmament. ### The Green Lion,

they also call a Mercury [☿] <note>The symbol for Mercury, representing the volatile principle of a substance</note>, or a Stone.

### To make an Oil from Coal, which has its virtues in Medicines.

Recipe [Take]: 1 pound of fat coal <note>Likely bituminous coal, known for its high oil and gas content</note>. Crush this very small into a powder. Then take an earthenware retort <note>A glass or ceramic vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note>, and put the crushed coal into it: distill it in the same manner as one makes a butter of Antimony [♁] <note>The symbol for Antimony; "butter of antimony" refers to antimony trichloride, but here indicates a specific distillation technique</note>, thus first some white mist will come over; and after that, a black Oil will come over. This must be kept in the Fire [Δ] <note>The triangle is the alchemical symbol for the element of Fire</note> so long, until all the Oil has come over. Then take the retort out of the oven when it is cold, and smash it to pieces. The *Caput Mortuum* <note>Latin: "dead head," the dregs or solid residue remaining after distillation</note> shall be crushed, and the Salt leached out of it with Water [▽] <note>The inverted triangle is the alchemical symbol for Water</note>. Then the Water [▽] shall be evaporated off again; thus the Salt remains *at the bottom* <note>original Latin: *in fundo*</note>.

Then take this Oil and this Salt, and put them into a glass retort, and distill both together; thus the Oil becomes very clear and pure. With this Oil, one can heal almost all evil external Wounds, for which otherwise no good recovery <note>The original "grond" implies a fundamental healing or a healthy base for the wound to close</note> can be obtained. I have experienced this myself. This Oil is not as sharp <note>Corrosive or caustic</note> as the oil of Antimony [♁], and moreover, there is not so much danger in its use. He who can now prepare the same shall also learn its use very well.

# or the Light of the Philosophers.

I have stated before that the Sun is fed and maintained by Salt; and this must hold firm: for from whatever God has brought forth a thing, by that same thing it is also sustained. Humans, made of earth, are by God’s grace fed by the fruits of the earth, by which their lives are maintained. The Heavens, made from Salt, give nourishment to the Sun and the Moon, and the other Planets, which is driven into the earth through the rays of the Sun, whereby all Minerals in the earth receive their growth, and the Herbs and Plants are also sustained, for the nourishment of Humans and Cattle. For the power of all Planets is in the *center* <note>original Latin: "Centrum"</note> of the Sun; and so it is also with Gold, in which all the powers of the Metals are hidden as if in a heap, in order to be able to heal all the others into a perfect body, just as Gold itself is; if one may believe the writings of the Philosophers.

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

Thus speaks Moses, Genesis in the first chapter: In the beginning God created the Heaven and the Earth. From this it is well understood that God made something beforehand, out of which these two great Buildings would be formed. I say then, that first a <term>Lump of Salt</term> <note>In alchemical cosmogony, the "Lump of Salt" (Sout-klomp) represents the "Materia Prima" or the primordial chaos—a physical substance through which the divine Word acted to create the world.</note> was made by God, which by the power of God was divided into 3 pieces; of which the Heaven, Sun, Moon, and Stars are formed out of the very purest <term>Spirit of Salt</term> <note>Here, "Spirit of Salt" does not refer to modern hydrochloric acid, but to a celestial, volatile essence that the author believes animates the heavens.</note>. From the second or coarsest part, the Earth is made; and the third part is the Water. And these are the three pieces that have come forth from the <term>Lump of Salt</term>, according to the will of God; and so the Heaven, and the Earth, with the Sea were created.

As proof of this, one will be able to see that when any thing is burned to ashes, nothing else shall remain but **Salt**, which is the first [substance] from which it had come forth.

The Water can become **Salt** again through the power of <term>Fire</term> <note>original: "△". In alchemy, the upward-pointing triangle is the symbol for the element of Fire.</note>: likewise, through the working of Nature, <term>Fire</term> can become a Water, and finally **Salt** and Earth.

Thus one can be changed into the other, and at last it shall be brought back again to that from which it first came forth, namely **Salt**.

### *Salt.*

Now you speak as if you were a philosopher: why then did you say that you should be the Master, since that title belongs to no one but me; for everything is preserved by Salt, and God made his Covenant with a Covenant of Salt <note>A reference to the biblical "Covenant of Salt" (Numbers 18:19), symbolizing an everlasting and incorruptible agreement, much as salt prevents decay.</note>. Yes, in me are all powers, both of Herbs, as well as Metals and Minerals. From my Flower <term>Flower</term> <note>In alchemy, "flowers" (flos) refer to the powdery substance or crystals produced through sublimation—the process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again.</note> a beautiful *Ruby-color* and *Tincture* <note>A "Tincture" is a potent medicinal or alchemical extract capable of transforming or "dyeing" other substances.</note> comes forth. For you have no Salt, or must receive it from me, in order to purify yourself with it: otherwise you are a deadly poison, for Mankind and for the Metals.

# The Green Lion,

now I say, that there is but one Salt of which the **Philosophers** have written; that the Salt of the **Metals** is their **Stone**. <note>The "Stone" refers to the Philosopher’s Stone, the ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to facilitate the transmutation of metals and the prolongation of life.</note>

# or the Light of the Philosophers.

# The Green Lion,

->Salt.<-

You, Antimony, with your following, make quite a remarkable company—one that can take the lives of both humans and beasts, which you can never restore. ### Closing Argument.

Kind reader, and true lover of Divine Nature, although I do not possess the <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> <note>The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life; here, the author humbly admits to being a seeker rather than a master.</note>, it is nonetheless permitted for me to bear witness to the works of the Lord, as far as they are known to me, just as I have done throughout my entire book. And to serve you even more, I wish to conclude here with some further Proverbs—

# The Green Lion,

...record the findings of the Philosophers, in which they, in my judgment, have taught us nothing but the truth.

I say that the Sun is the light of all the Planets; and so too is Gold the luster of all the Metals. Wine is the Fountain of the earth, which is distilled on high by the power of the Lord, and is a drink for Mankind and for the Metals: this is a sublimated water, which contains <term>fire</term> <note>The original text uses the triangle symbol ∆ for fire.</note> and spirit within itself. Its Salt is a Medicine for Mankind, as well as for all other Salts: through it, all evil Salts are brought to a good Medicine; of which I have treated in my previous writings.

The Salt of Wine, and the Salt of Gold, with the Salt of Mankind, contain more powers within them than are known to me or the entire world; therefore I cannot write with full certainty of these three.

*Count Bernard* <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist. His "Fountain" is a famous allegory for the alchemical solvent or the process of dissolution.</note> recounts that he found a Fountain, into which he let his golden book fall. Whether this might be that fine Fountain, is for us to consider.

The Philosophers all speak with one voice, saying that the entire <term>Art</term> <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, specifically the "Great Work" of transforming base matter into perfection.</note> is hidden in the Sun and the Moon, and we must also agree to this: but the most important matter here is what they intended by a Sun or Moon...

<summary>The author compares the Sun and Gold as the lights of their respective realms and discusses the alchemical properties of "Wine" (spirit) and "Salt." He references the famous allegory of Bernard of Trevisan's fountain and notes that the secret of alchemy lies in understanding what the "Sun and Moon" truly represent.</summary>

<keywords>Alchemy, Sun and Moon, Gold, Salt of Wine, Bernard of Trevisan, Medicine, Elements, The Art</keywords>

--- Page 278 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion regarding the true identity of the "Sun" and "Moon" in alchemical texts. The opening word "have" completes the sentence from the preceding page.</meta>

### or the Light of the Philosophers.

...they must have; for sometimes by their Sun they understand their Salt, and then again their Vinegar, or even the Blood of the Red Lion <note>The "Red Lion" is a common alchemical symbol for the philosopher's stone or a specific state of mercury/gold during the process.</note>. Their Moon they also call a Virgin’s milk, a *Sal Armoniac* <note>Sal ammoniac, or ammonium chloride, frequently used as a flux or to dissolve metals.</note>, a Salt of the Metals, and by many other names. But I say that the same, in my judgment, cannot be anything other than the Salt of the earth, which is produced by the Metals.

The Ancient Sages give us much account of all these matters in their Books: for they say that when one works without Salt in this Art <note>The "Art" refers to the Great Work of Alchemy.</note>, one shall never, unto eternal days, reach any perfection. This same thing is told to us by *Basil* <note>Basil Valentine, a famous 15th-century alchemist and author of "The Twelve Keys."</note> in his 4th Key. Another Philosopher says: He who works without Salt, walks like a Blind man without a Guide; and like one who would wish to climb to Heaven without a step or ladder, which is impossible. They also say that in the Sun and in the Salt is hidden everything that we need in this Art.

One reads in *Arnold of Villanova* <note>Arnaldus de Villa Nova (c. 1240–1311), a major medieval authority on medicine and alchemy.</note>, chapter 7, that in <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note>, and in <term>Gold</term> <note>original: ☉, the symbol for the Sun</note>, the tincture <note>A substance capable of imparting color or quality, used to describe the transforming power of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> for Gold is found; and in <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note> is that for Silver, in which the entire Art consists. We can also well say that in Gold and in <term>Silver</term> <note>original: ☽, the symbol for the Moon</note>, with <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note>, the entire Art exists, just as *Arnold* tells us; but the reason for the <term>Sun</term> <note>original: ☉</note> and...

<summary>The author decodes the metaphorical language of alchemists, explaining that the "Sun," "Moon," and "Salt" represent specific chemical states or catalysts. # The Green Lion,

How to prepare it <note>Referring back to the tincture or specific alchemical substance mentioned at the end of the previous page.</note> has been concealed by him: and I believe that the same must be done by the Salt of the Old Philosophers, which they also call their <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note>; but I can write no further of it than what is yet known to me: nevertheless, I will not doubt but that it is a special Salt, which many often hold in their hands; only we do not yet know how to use the same for this Art <note>The "Art" is the traditional term for the practice of alchemy.</note>.

In *Bernardus* <note>Likely Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous alchemist often quoted regarding the "oneness" of the alchemical substance.</note> one reads the following words: *That they may very well remain silent, who wish to extract another* <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note> *than that which is in the Ferment, or the Red Servant; and wish to seek out another* <term>Water</term> <note>original: 🜄</note>. From this it is sufficiently clear to conclude that their <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note> must be Salt; yet where it lies buried, *Sendivogius* <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a Polish alchemist who taught that a "life-force" or "nitre" was hidden in the air and soil.</note> and *Rhumelius* <note>Johann Pharamund Rhumelius (1597–1661), a physician and alchemical writer.</note> have written, whose own words I have cited in various places in this Book. They say that this Salt comes from Heaven into the Earth, and there takes on a body, whether it be good or evil; and this they call the <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note> of the Philosophers.

We find daily that all fruits of the Land come forth by the power of Heaven driven into the Earthly realm, as with a moist Rain or Dew, which then at last become a Salt and <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note>. ### ...or the Light of the Philosophers.

Salt? You will become aware of this when you place a piece of wood in the <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note> to burn; for then you shall see, once the <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note> has truly taken hold of the wood, that the coarsest <term>Water</term> <note>original: ▽</note> will run out from it; and the second, or the lightest part, which is called <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note>, goes off as smoke into the air, and the ashes, in which the Salt is, remain below. He who can now catch that fine smoke, and unite an <term>Earth</term> <note>original: ♁</note> with fine Salt, shall obtain that which I have taught in this Book of the *Oak Tree*.

So it is also with the Grapevine; when you have cut off the fine fruits and burned the vines with <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note>, then the <term>Earth</term> <note>original: ♁</note> and <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note> shall fly away in smoke, and in the ashes there is then a Salt, which is very similar in taste to common <term>Salt</term> <note>original: ☐</note>.

NB. If one burns common <term>Earth</term> <note>original: ♁</note> in the <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note> with <term>Gold</term> <note>original: ☉</note>, then these, melted in the air, shall become an Oil. This is very fiery upon the tongue. If 5 or 6 drops of this are put into a small rummer of wine, it shall make the wine sweet and pleasant of taste; and it is good against the Stone and the Gravel <note>Historical terms for kidney and bladder stones.</note>. The <term>Gold</term> <note>original: ☉</note>, burned by a piece of charcoal and then melted into an oil in the air, also makes the wine red and sweet. Thus the Salts, which are reborn through the <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note>, take on the nature of <term>Water</term> <note>original: ▽</note>; and the powers of the <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note> [reside] in the Salt,
