->...or the Light of the Philosophers.<-

Q 2

God first created the Heaven, and thereafter the Earth. The Earth now was desolate and empty, and the Spirit of the Lord hovered upon the waters. From this it can be sufficiently gathered that this Earth is a dried-out Salt, which is again moistened by the Spirit of God, which hovered upon the waters: for when the mass of Heaven, Earth, and Water was still together, it was but a whole lump of Salt, which thereafter, through the power of God, <term>dissolved</term> <note>original: geſolveert</note> into a murky and desolate Sea, out of which everything was made through the Word of God.

Thus one can easily see what the <term>first matter</term> <note>original: materie; refers to the "Prima Materia," the original fundamental substance of the universe</note> may be, namely Salt, in which the <term>Water</term> <note>original: ▽</note> and <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note> had hidden themselves. This shall be found when a piece of wood comes to the <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △</note>, that it shall burn, where it is nothing more than a <term>coagulated</term> <note>original: gecoaguleert; thickened or solidified</note> <term>Water</term> <note>original: ▽</note>; for which reason almost its entire <term>Body</term> <note>original: Corpus</note> flies away into the air, while in its ashes there is still some Salt, which can be used to make glass. The smoke is its <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; the volatile, spirit-like principle in alchemy</note>, which likewise still has some Salt within it. He who now has understanding can easily comprehend how everything has come forth from Salt, and must return to Salt again; just as God spoke to Man; *Earth you are, and to earth you shall return* <note>original: "Aerde zijt gy, en ſult weder tot aerde worden"; a reference to Genesis 3:19</note>, which is actually Salt: for all things have in the Salt...

# The Green Lion,

[...their] rest, because they first proceeded from the Salt.

Man lives by means of Salt, and without Salt, men would have to rot from within throughout their entire body, since it is made of Salt; and this is the earth from which it was taken <note>The author alludes to the biblical "dust to dust," but interprets "dust" specifically as chemical Salt, the preservative principle that prevents corruption/rotting</note>.

This shall yet be found when one takes a *Body* <note>original: Corpus</note> that has decayed in a coffin and has been consumed by the air; therein one can see what it was made of, namely Salt. The remaining <term>residue</term> <note>original: feces; in alchemical practice, this refers to the solid dregs or "dead head" left over after a substance has been burned or distilled</note> may be burned, and treated as one does with other Salts, until the entire body is brought back to a Salt, from which it had originated.

In this manner, all things are to be reduced to a Salt, by Nature, through the passage of time.

Now, enough has finally been treated regarding Salt for this time: but it seems to me that there is yet another Salt currently lacking in the world, namely, the Salt of Knowledge and of Love: for ignorance brings everything into confusion and into ruin; and a lack of love causes everything to be destroyed through strife and discord; whereas otherwise Unity would create strength <note>A reference to the Dutch national motto "Eendracht maakt macht" (Unity makes strength)</note>: and if this could be found among us, then there would be shown to us that which is related in the <term>Book of Revelation</term> <note>original: Openbaring</note> chapter 1, verses 12, 13, 14, 16, with the following...

*Green Lion, where to find it. 40 it is a salt of the metals, brought forth by the Mercury of the Sages.* 41 <note>The "Green Lion" is a famous alchemical symbol, often representing vitriol or a specific state of raw mineral matter.</note>
*Green Oven.* 118 <note>Likely referring to a specific type of furnace or a state within the chemical vessel.</note>

## H.

*Splendid Color and Medicine. 192 Tincture.* 195 <term>Tincture; a potent medicinal or transmuting extract.</term>
*Heavens made from salt give nourishment to the Sun and Moon.* 203 <note>The Sun and Moon are standard alchemical symbols for Gold and Silver.</note>
*The salt of wine, and the salt of gold, with the salt of man, contains more power than anyone can understand.* 238 <note>The "salt of man" often refers to substances derived from the human body, such as urea, which were highly valued in certain alchemical traditions.</note>
*How all salts are to be reduced to a water.* 213, 214

## I.

*In the Sun and salts a treasure is hidden, which is sought by many people.* 206
*In the center of the Sun are all the powers of the other planets.* 209 <note>The Sun was viewed as the heart of the celestial and terrestrial systems, containing the virtues of all other metals/planets.</note>
*Iron is the most necessary mineral. Preface p. 2*
*Job treats of silver and gold, iron and copper. 210, 211* <note>A reference to the Biblical Book of Job, which mentions ancient mining and metallurgy.</note>

## K.

*Lime makes all sour things sweet.* 187 <note>original: "Kalk." This refers to the process of neutralization, where alkaline lime (calcium oxide) counteracts acidity.</note>

*Tincture of the Sun. 208* <note>original: "Tinctura Solis." Sol (the Sun) is the alchemical name for Gold; a tincture is a concentrated medicinal extract.</note>
*Tincture from Verdigris. 195*
*Turpeth Mineral. 58* <note>original: "Turbith Minerael." A basic mercury sulfate used as a powerful purgative and in the treatment of syphilis.</note>

->V.<-

*Venus plays between Saturn and the Moon. 71* <note>In alchemical allegory, this refers to Copper (Venus) reacting or being refined in relation to Lead (Saturn) and Silver (the Moon/Luna).</note>
*Putrefaction of the Metals. 133* <note>The stage of "breaking down" or "blackening" (nigredo) where the material is allowed to rot so it can be reborn in a higher form.</note>
*Various colors from Mercury. 160*
*Four Metals presented to us in the Holy Scripture; but of the three others nothing is written there.*
*88, 94* <note>The "seven metals" were traditionally linked to the seven planets; the author notes that only four are explicitly named in the Bible.</note>
*Fire from Mercury. 159*
*Fiery spirit from Limestone. 184 from Tartar and Sal Ammoniac with Vinegar. 194* <note>original: "Wÿnsteen" (Tartar) and "Sal Armoniac" (Ammonium chloride). These were standard reagents for creating volatile "spirits."</note>
*Vitriol of the Ancients is to be obtained in all places. 127* <note>Vitriol (metal sulfates) was considered a universal starting material; its name was often used as an acronym for "Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem" (Visit the interior of the earth; by rectification you will find the hidden stone).</note>

<summary>This index page covers the letters S, T, and V, detailing alchemical procedures involving salts, the "Steel" of Sendivogius, and various tinctures. It highlights the overlap between metallurgical transmutation and the preparation of medicines for conditions like kidney stones.</summary>
<keywords>Salt, Sulfur, Sendivogius, Spirit of Salt, Philosopher's Stone, Verdigris, Tincture of Gold, Turpeth Mineral, Venus, Saturn, Vitriol</keywords>

# I N D E X .

*Vitriol of the Metals.* 145 <note>Vitriol refers to various metallic sulfates, often used to create potent acids for dissolving metals.</note>
*Bird of the midnight.* 50, 54 <note>Likely an alchemical allegory for the "black sun" or the state of matter during the "nigredo" (blackening) stage of the Work.</note>
*From everything that is burnt to ashes, a Salt can be made through Air*
*and Water.* 112
*To make a King from Antimony and Iron.* 216 <note>original: "Konink." This refers to the "Star-Regulus of Antimony," a crystalline metallic form produced by smelting antimony ore with iron; it was highly prized by alchemists for its star-like patterns.</note>
*Dried-out Alchemists.* 21 <note>Likely a derogatory or cautionary reference to failed practitioners or the exhaustion of one's resources.</note>
*Dried-out Salt is Earth.* 243
*From Salt everything has come forth, and everything must return*
*to Salt again.* 243

-> W. <-

*From what the Metals and Minerals have come*
*forth.* 99
*Waters are of many kinds.* 42, 45, 46
*Water is the principal Element.* 189
*To cure dropsy.* 167, 216 <note>original: "Waterzucht." A historical medical term for edema or swelling caused by excess fluid in the body.</note>
*What God has not made, can hardly be brought about*
*by us.* 66
*Wine is at once a Water and a Fire.* 196 <note>This refers to the liquid nature of wine (water) combined with its volatile, flammable spirit (fire/alcohol).</note>
*Tartar, and Sal Ammoniac, with Vinegar, give a*
*fiery spirit.* 194 <note>original: "Wijnsteen." Tartar is the potassium bitartrate found in wine barrels; Sal Ammoniac is ammonium chloride.</note>
*Tartar has Fire, Salt, and Air.* 242
*Tartar calcined to a green Stone.* 213 <note>Calcination involves heating a substance to high temperatures to transform its chemical properties.</note>
*White spirit from Mercury Sublimate and the mineral*
*of Antimony.* 101 <note>original: "Merkurius Sublimaet." Corrosive sublimate, a toxic mercuric chloride used as a powerful reagent.</note>
*To cleanse wounds. 186 and to heal. 187, 193*
*Wondrous Fire from the Metals.* 139
*Dwelling of Saturn. 249, 83 of Mercury. 14* <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the planetary symbol for Lead, and Mercury for Quicksilver. Their "dwellings" refer to their chemical or celestial origins.</note>

-> Z. <-

*To make Sand into a Glass, indeed into a Stone.* 201

<meta>This page follows the completion of the alphabetical index from the previous page. It transitions from the textual reference list to a visual and symbolic key, providing definitions for the alchemical and astrological characters used throughout the manuscript. This glossary is essential for interpreting the recipes and chemical procedures mentioned in earlier sections.</meta>

## EXPLANATION
### O F  T H E
# SYMBOLS.

$\odot$, *Gold, the Sun, Gold.* <note>original: "Aurum, Sol, Gout." In alchemy, gold was synonymous with the Sun.</note>
$\leftmoon$, *Silver, the Moon, Silver.* <note>original: "Argentum, Luna, Silver." Silver was associated with the lunar qualities of the Moon.</note>
$\♂$, *Mars, Iron.* <note>original: "Mars, Yſer." The symbol for the god of war represented the strength of iron.</note>
$\♀$, *Venus, Copper.* <note>original: "Venus, Koper."</note>
$\♃$, *Jupiter, Tin.* <note>original: "Jupiter, Tin."</note>
$\♄$, *Saturn, Lead.* <note>original: "Saturnus, Lood."</note>
$\☿$, *Mercury, Quicksilver.* <note>original: "Merkurius, Quiksilver."</note>
$\circ$, *Alum.* <note>A mineral salt used in dyeing and as a preservative.</note>
<image-desc>Circle with vertical bar</image-desc>, *Saltpeter.* <note>original: "Salpeter." Potassium nitrate, a key ingredient in gunpowder and a potent chemical solvent.</note>
$\ominus$, *Common Salt.* <note>original: "Gemeen Sout." Sodium chloride.</note>
$\oplus$, *Vitriol, Copper-red.* <note>original: "Vitriool, Koper-rood." Vitriol refers to metallic sulfates; "copper-red" likely refers to a specific calcined or impure state of iron or copper sulfate.</note>
$\oplus$, *Rock Salt, Stone-Salt.* <note>original: "Sal Gemmæ, Steen-Sout." Naturally occurring halite crystals.</note>
<image-desc>Square with cross on top</image-desc>, *Calcined Tartar.* <note>original: "Gecalcineerde Wijn-ſteen." Potassium carbonate produced by heating the sediment from wine barrels.</note>
$\square$, *Tartar, Raw Tartar.* <note>original: "Tartarum, Rouwe Wijn-ſteen." The crude potassium bitartrate found in wine casks.</note>
<image-desc>Triangle with cross on top</image-desc>, *Sulfur, Brimstone.* <note>original: "Sulphur, Swavel."</note>
$*$, *Sal Ammoniac.* <note>original: "Sal Armoniac." Ammonium chloride, often used as a flux in metallurgy.</note>
$\infty$, *Arsenic, Rat-poison.* <note>original: "Arsenicum, Rotte-kruit." Arsenic trioxide was historically used as a pesticide.</note>
$\text{\oast}$, *Antimony.* <note>original: "Antimonium." A brittle, silvery-white metalloid.</note>
<image-desc>Circle with cross on top</image-desc>, *Verdigris, or Roman Vitriol.* <note>original: "Spaens Groen, of Roomſche-Vitriool." Verdigris is the green pigment formed on copper; Roman Vitriol usually refers to high-quality blue copper sulfate.</note>
<image-desc>Cross on a horizontal line base</image-desc>, *Vinegar.* <note>original: "Aſijn."</note>
$\times$, *Distilled Vinegar.* <note>original: "Gediſtilleerden Aſijn." A more concentrated form of acetic acid.</note>
$\approx$, *Rainwater.* <note>original: "Regen-water." Highly valued by alchemists for its perceived purity compared to well or river water.</note>
$\Delta$, *Fire.* <note>original: "Vyer." One of the four classical elements.</note>
$\nabla$, *Water.* <note>One of the four classical elements.</note>
$\nabla$, *Nitric Acid, Strong Water.* <note>original: "Aqua Fortis, Sterk Water." A powerful acid used to dissolve silver and other metals.</note>
℞, *Aqua Regia.* <note>original: "Aqua Regis" or "Royal Water." A mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids capable of dissolving "royal" gold.</note>
<image-desc>Open bottomless square</image-desc>, *Body, Calcined Salt.* <note>original: "Corpus, Gecalcineert Sout." In alchemy, the "body" refers to the solid, fixed remains of a substance.</note>
$\libra$, *Spirit, Essence.* <note>original: "Spiritus, Geeſt." The volatile or gaseous part of a substance.</note>
$\Psi$, *Calx.* <note>original: "Kalk." The powdery residue or oxide left after a metal or mineral has been thoroughly roasted or calcined.</note>

<summary>This page serves as a reference key for alchemical symbols, defining the planetary characters for metals and the specific shorthand icons used for various chemical substances and elements like Aqua Fortis and sulfur.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical symbols, planetary metals, chemical glossary, Aqua Fortis, Aqua Regia, calcination, minerals, historical chemistry</keywords>
